# Deferred-Anchor Registry — Production

> The canonical source of truth for every `D-*` anchor cited in `src/` but
> deferred to a future cycle. The leading underscore sorts this file before
> the numbered plans in directory listings — call this out as the registry,
> not a plan. Per-plan §3.1 rows are still the preferred home for anchors
> whose feature area maps cleanly onto a plan; this file catches the orphans
> + cross-cutting anchors that don't belong to any single plan.
>
> **CI/pre-commit guard** (`scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.{sh,ps1}`): every
> `D-*` identifier appearing in `src/` MUST resolve to a row in ANY of the three
> registry documents (a closed anchor's row lives in `-done.md`, and a citation of
> it must still resolve)
> OR a citation in any `.plans/*.md`. Pre-fix this recurred TWICE
> (memory: "the same gap silent-failure caught for MULTI-PAGE-GOT").
> The guard makes the system enforce the discipline — same shape as the
> agnosticism veto + the `CallConv` static_assert.
>
> **★ ANCHOR-NAME RULE — READ BEFORE INVENTING A `D-*` NAME (documented 2026-07-28, TF-C81; it was
> load-bearing and unwritten until it bit).** The guard's regex is
> `D-[A-Z0-9_]+(-[A-Z0-9_]+){2,}` — **`D-` plus THREE OR MORE `-`-separated segments is a
> DEFERRED-WORK ANCHOR and MUST resolve to a row here; a TWO-segment `D-*` name (`D-OPT`) is an
> informal feature label the guard ignores.** So hyphenating a COMPOUND FEATURE WORD silently changes
> the identifier's CLASS: `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYS-INLINE` tripped the guard and had to be renamed
> `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE` to match its sibling `D-CSUBSET-NOINLINE`. The failure surfaces as
> “missing row”, which misdirects you into WRITING a duplicate row instead of FIXING the name.
> **Spell a compound feature word as ONE segment (`ALWAYSINLINE`, `NOINLINE`); reserve hyphens for
> genuine name components; when in doubt, match the sibling anchor already in this file.** Full
> write-up: [[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-NAME-SEGMENT-COUNT-GATE]].
>
> **V2-4.X / full-C ownership (2026-06-10):** the C-language-feature deferral
> CLUSTER — modulo codegen (`D-CSUBSET-MOD-OP-CODEGEN`, …), primitive widths
> (`D-LANG-PLATFORM-DEPENDENT-PRIMITIVE-WIDTH`), linkage/visibility syntax

> V2-4.X / [`23-full-c-plan`](./23-full-c-plan%20-%20tbd.md)** (c-subset → full
> C23). Each closes inside the relevant FC phase (see the plan §3.1).
>
> **NOT in this cluster — already shipped, do NOT re-claim as new FC work**
> (caught by the 2026-06-10 plan-lock accuracy review). Two genuinely-CLOSED
> anchors: `D-CSUBSET-DIVISION-OP-CODEGEN` (✅ 2026-06-04 — signed division shipped;
> the modulo successor `D-CSUBSET-MOD-OP-CODEGEN` ✅ since closed too — FC1 cycle 1, 2026-06-10, above) and `D-FFI-EXTERN-CALL-DISPATCH`

> Individual rows are NOT rewritten here yet (the plan is the live map until a

> **★★ MEASUREMENT CAVEAT — READ BEFORE QUOTING ANY “this will clear N errors” NUMBER IN THIS FILE (2026-07-28, macOS-arm64 predefine-probe cycle).** DSS **abandons a TU at its first hard parse failure**, so a corpus error count is a count of the errors REACHED, never of the defects PRESENT. Clearing an early blocker therefore EXPOSES later ones that were never reached, and a drop in the total is NOT a count of defects fixed. Worked example from this cycle: the 189-TU sqlite build went **213 → 80** total errors when architecture + toolchain predefines were both supplied — that is **NOT 133 defects fixed**; the residual 80 is a NEWLY-EXPOSED population whose composition was unknown until separately measured. It is equally possible for a real fix to make the total go UP (this cycle measured exactly that: toolchain-identity macros ALONE took 213 → 216). Every forward estimate of the form “closing X clears N errors” in any row below MUST be read with this caveat, and every new one MUST carry it. See [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] for the citable class row.
>
> **Schema (one row per anchor)**:
> ```
> | Anchor | Priority | Status | Trigger | Closing work | Cross-refs |
> ```
>
> When an anchor closes, replace its row's status with `✅ CLOSED <date>`,
> cite the commit/SymbolId, and **MOVE the row into**
> [`_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md) —
> `scripts/apply-registry-row/apply-registry-row.py` does both in one step, and
> `check-anchor-balance` fails the tree if the move is skipped. Don't DELETE the
> row: the audit trail is
> load-bearing for future regressions.
>

> (C 6.9.2 tentative definitions — still fail-loud, trigger-gated). [That backlog

> re-probe fired its trigger — see its row below.]

> ⚠⚠ **THIS REGISTRY IS ONE OF THREE, AND IT HOLDS ONLY PRODUCTION WORK THAT IS STILL
> OPEN (333 rows).** Not one row here is finished. A row that closes is MOVED OUT.
> Its siblings are [`_deferred-anchor-registry-harness.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-harness.md) -- the other half of the
> live work -- and [`_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md),
> the archive, which already holds 1114 closed production rows lifted out of this file.
>
> **★★★ THE MOVE-ON-CLOSE RULE (operator, 2026-09-01).** *"the
> `_deferred-anchor-registry-{harness|production}.md` is a list of remaining items, that
> always delete a done item and put into `_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md` once
> finished."* So this file answers exactly one question -- **what is left** -- and a
> reader who wants the count of remaining work can take the length of the table below.
> Before 2026-09-01 this file held 1,555 closed rows against 523 open ones: three
> quarters of every orientation read was finished work, and the priority was buried
> under its own audit trail.
> ⚠ **THE AUDIT TRAIL IS NOT DELETED, IT IS RELOCATED.** The preamble above still
> says never to delete a closed row and that is still true -- `done.md` is where it goes.
> ⚠ **A CLOSED ROW IN THIS FILE IS A REFUSAL**, not untidiness:
> `check-anchor-balance` fails the tree for it. Close a row with
> `scripts/apply-registry-row/apply-registry-row.py`, which performs the move itself --
> hand-editing the table is how the two halves drift.
>
> **THE LINE BETWEEN PRODUCTION AND HARNESS, so a borderline row is decided by a rule and
> not by taste:**
> **PRODUCTION** is a defect a USER OF THE COMPILER could hit -- it is in the shipped binary
> or in the config that binary reads (front end, preprocessor, semantics, HIR/MIR/LIR,
> optimiser, assembler, linker, object formats, FFI surface, diagnostics, CLI, emitted
> runtime).
> **HARNESS** is a defect only WE can hit -- tests, gates, guards, the cycle machinery,
> plans, scripts, carriages, CI, the example corpus AS a corpus.
> ⚠ `D-CONFIG-*` and `D-DIAG-*` are PRODUCTION deliberately: a `.lang/.target/.format.json`
> document IS the compiler's behaviour in this architecture, and a diagnostic is its output to a
> user. Filing either as tooling would hide real conformance defects in the harness pile, which is
> the exact thing that split exists to stop.
> ⚠ A row's bucket follows the DEFECT, never the instrument that found it: a corpus example that
> reveals a codegen bug files under codegen.
>
> ★ **RESOLUTION READS ALL THREE FILES; ORIENTATION READS ONLY THE TWO WORKING ONES.**
> A `D-*` cited in `src/` must resolve wherever its row now lives, so every guard that
> RESOLVES a citation globs `_deferred-anchor-registry*.md`. Every instrument that asks
> *what is left* -- `burndown-queue`, the cycle's priority pick -- reads production and
> harness ONLY. Reading the archive to choose work is how a closed row got recommended
> three times in this project's history.

## Anchor Index

**Row shape: EXACTLY six cells — `Anchor`, `Priority`, `Status`, `Trigger`, `Closing work`, `Cross-refs`.**
★★★ **`Priority` IS `P0`..`P5` AND `Status` IS ONE OF `✅ CLOSED` / `🟠 OPEN` / `⏳ GATED` — a three-value controlled vocabulary, added
2026-09-01 on the operator's instruction: *"add columns for priority and status ... this way
we always have clean statuses."* Before that the verdict was the FIRST GLYPH of a prose blob
that also carried the trigger, the history and the retraction, and every reader had to agree
where the verdict stopped — which is precisely the agreement that failed four times in
`check-anchor-balance`'s own comment history.
⚠ **THE STATUS CELL KEEPS ITS GLYPH, AND THE GLYPH IS THE CONTRACT.** A row is CLOSED iff its
status cell OPENS with ✅ after stripping `*_ ` — the complement defined, never the variants,
so a glyph nobody has thought of yet counts OPEN. A column holding the bare WORD `CLOSED`
would make that test false for every closed row at once. The word is for the reader.
⚠ **`Priority` IS A DECLARATION, NOT A SIEVE RESULT.** `burndown-queue` seeds it and then
READS it; its own docstring warns that a band is *"a sort key, not a verdict"* because a
census built from that keyword sieve reported 103 where the truth was 4. Correct the cell and
the correction survives, which re-running a sieve never does.
★ **DO NOT HAND-WRITE A ROW.** `scripts/anchors/anchors.py` (and its `write-anchor` /
`set-anchor` / `read-anchor` / `read-anchors` launchers) takes the FIELDS and produces the row,
so a wrapped id, an unescaped pipe and a wrong cell count are all inexpressible.
A row with fewer cells shifts every following cell one column LEFT; a row with more
has the surplus SILENTLY DROPPED by the renderer. Neither is visible when reading the
raw text, so the count is load-bearing — see `D-PLANS-REGISTRY-MALFORMED-ROW-CELLS`.

Two authoring rules follow from that, both verified against the GitHub renderer:
- **A raw `|` splits a cell EVEN INSIDE BACKTICKS.** To quote an expression, regex or
  alternation containing one, write `\|` — the backslash-escaped form is the *only*
  one that survives inside a code span (an HTML entity does **not**).
- **` ═══ ` inside a cell marks a seam where two cells were joined** when a row
  authored to a wider schema was converged onto these four columns. It is a joint in
  the prose, not a column boundary.

⚠ **APPEND AT END-OF-TABLE, NEVER AT END-OF-FILE.** This registry once carried a
second `## Anchor Index (continued)` table, which existed only because rows had been
appended past the Allowlist over many cycles; its header declared TWO columns, so every
fuller row silently lost its `Closing work` and `Cross-refs`. The 2026-08-25
production/harness split merged both tables, so each file now has exactly ONE index and
the accident cannot recur here -- but the rule that prevents it still has to be obeyed.

| Anchor | Priority | Status | Trigger | Closing work | Cross-refs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D-CSUBSET-CONST-EVAL-CHAR-SIGNEDNESS` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★★ RE-MEASURED END-TO-END 2026-07-28 (TF-C75 fold), AND THE ROW'S OWN CLAIMS WERE PART RIGHT, PART REFUTED, PART UNOBSERVABLE. Read this block BEFORE the WAS-text below, which contains a claim that measurement does NOT support.** ⚠ **CORRECTION — THE SIGNEDNESS CLAIM IS A LABELLED SUSPECT, NOT A FACT.** The WAS-text asserts that `intKindInfo(Char) = {32,false}` makes a folded `(char)0x80 < 0` fold WRONG "on ALL targets, i.e. wrong on x86_64/pe64". **An end-to-end probe against `/usr/bin/clang` on `arm64:macho64-arm64-darwin-exec` did NOT reproduce the signedness half in ANY reachable position** — expression, runtime-variable, static-initializer and enum-constant forms of `(char)-1 < 0` all fold CORRECTLY and match clang. Do NOT re-propose the signedness claim as established; the refutation is kept here so it is not rediscovered as news. ✅ **WHAT *IS* MEASURED AND LIVE IS THE WIDTH HALF — and its true scope is NARROWER AND SHARPER than either the WAS-text or the relaying brief stated.** Measured 2026-07-28 with the shipped CLI (`build/bin/dss/dss-code-prime`, post-TF-C75) against `/usr/bin/clang`, target `arm64:macho64-arm64-darwin-exec`, identical in `--config=debug` AND `--config=release`: **(a)** `static int g = (char)200; g == -56` → clang **5**, DSS **6** ✗ — DSS keeps **200**. **(b)** function-local `static int g = (char)200` → same divergence ✗ (so it is not file-scope-specific). **(c)** `static int g = (char)(100+100)` → same ✗ (so it is the CAST, not literal folding). **(d)** `static int g = (char)300; g == 44` → DSS ✗ (keeps 300 — this is the probe that isolates WIDTH, since 8-bit truncation gives 44 under EITHER signedness). ✔ **AND THE CONTROLS, which are what make this actionable — every one of these is CORRECT today:** `(signed char)200` ✓ (→ -56), `(unsigned char)300` ✓ (→ 44), `(short)70000` ✓ (→ 4464), `(char)200` in an ORDINARY EXPRESSION ✓, in an ENUM constant ✓, in an ARRAY-SIZE constant expression ✓. **So the defect is `TypeKind::Char` SPECIFICALLY, in the STATIC/GLOBAL-INITIALIZER const-eval path ONLY.** `signed char`→`I8`={8,true} and `unsigned char`→`U8`={8,false} are both right; only the bare-`char` row is wrong, and it is wrong in BOTH fields. ⭐ **THE MECHANISM IS FULLY PINNED — no guesswork left for the closing cycle:** ✔TREE `wrapToIntTarget` (`const_eval_arith.hpp:631-640`) returns `v` UNCHANGED when `bits >= 64`, else masks to `bits` and sign-extends iff `isSigned`. With `{32,false}`: 200 & 0xFFFFFFFF = 200 and no sign extension ⇒ 200. With the correct `{8,true}` ⇒ -56 ✓; with `{8,false}` (right for linux-aarch64) ⇒ 200, which is also correct there. Both measured values fall straight out of the two wrong fields. ★★ **WHY THE SIGNEDNESS HALF IS CURRENTLY *UNOBSERVABLE* RATHER THAN MERELY UNCONFIRMED — this explains the failed reproduction and is the single most useful sentence for the closing cycle:** while `bits` is 32, NO end-to-end probe can distinguish signed from unsigned, because a value that survives un-truncated is identical under both. `(char)200` yields 200 under `{32,true}`, `{32,false}` AND `{8,false}` — only `{8,true}` gives -56. **The width bug MASKS the signedness bug.** Therefore: fix the WIDTH first, THEN re-measure signedness — and expect a NEW divergence to appear at that moment rather than treating its current absence as evidence of correctness. An agent who "verifies" signedness today and finds it clean has measured the mask, not the code. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED** — measured end-to-end on the live macho-arm64 leg, in both build configs, on legal C that clang compiles correctly. Closing work: make `intKindInfo`'s `Char` arm carry the correct **8-bit width** (unconditional — `char` is 8 bits in every data model DSS ships) and a **target/format-resolved sign**, threaded to all FOUR consumers, NONE of which is target-aware today: ✔TREE `const_eval.cpp:343` + `:415` (HIR const-eval — has `EvalOptions`, but `intKindInfo` does not take it), `const_eval_arith.hpp:665` (`bitIntOperandType` → the `_BitInt` UAC bignum chain), and the SEPARATE MIR optimizer `opt/passes/const_fold.cpp:251`, whose `normalizeToType` (`:244`) holds only an interner — no target, no format. **That last one is why this is a PLUMBING problem and not a one-liner**, and it is the reason a naive `EvalOptions`-only fix is a half-measure that leaves the optimizer blind. ⭐ **WHAT TF-C75 HANDS THIS ROW — the thing it was blocked on: ONE canonical producer, `effectiveCharIsUnsigned(target, format)`** (`src/program/compile_pipeline.cpp:86`, wired at `:613`). NOTE: the macho non-libSystem dylib-link witness (was zlib_roundtrip's darwin arm) also moves to the harness (libtcl.dylib on the macos leg). (ARCHITECTURAL RULE, user-directed 2026-07-24) — shipped-lib descriptors are for OS libraries ONLY; every OTHER library must be consumed by PARSING ITS REAL HEADERS.** User verbatim: *"tcl and any other external lib should NOT be shipped libs, only OS libs must be in shipped libs, the other libs should be parsed"*. Today DSS ships `tcl.json` + `zlib.json` (and resolves `<tcl.h>`/`<zlib.h>` through them), and the arm64 testfixture recipe carries the standing warning *"do NOT `-I` the real tcl/zlib headers — a real header on `-I` makes DSS PARSE it (P0009)"*. That warning IS the defect: the descriptors exist BECAUSE the front end cannot parse those headers, so a hand-maintained JSON is standing in for real C parsing. That is a workaround (§A.2) and it is unsound in a second way — a descriptor is a hand-transcribed ABI claim that can silently drift from the installed library's real headers (`#47`-class type-identity risk), whereas parsing the actual header is self-correcting by construction. **The boundary:** an OS/platform surface (libc, syscalls, Win32/kernel APIs, the C runtime) legitimately ships a descriptor — its ABI is the platform contract and headers may be absent or compiler-specific. A THIRD-PARTY library the user builds and links (Tcl, zlib, and anything like them) must be `-I`-included and PARSED like any other C the compiler compiles. | Trigger: UNCONDITIONAL — it is a standing rule for all new library surfaces (do NOT add another third-party descriptor), and it gates the `real-examples/c/sqlite` harness direction. Closing: (1) root-cause the P0009 failures parsing real `tcl.h` + `zlib.h` and fix them as ordinary front-end gaps (each its own anchor); (2) switch the testfixture recipe to real `-I` includes; (3) DELETE `tcl.json`/`zlib.json` from shippedLibs, keeping only OS surfaces; (4) a red-on-disable witness = the testfixture builds against the REAL headers with no descriptor present. Expect this to surface genuine C the front end cannot yet handle — that is the point, and it is the same "root-cause from the CODE, never a workaround" discipline the char/int→ptr cycles followed. ★★ **AGNOSTICISM IS BINDING HERE (user-directed: "parse the headers in an agnostic way (follow the bar)") — §A.1 applies with no exceptions.** Every fix that makes a real header parse MUST be a GENERIC C front-end capability, never library-aware: NO `if (header == "tcl.h")`, no per-library include arm, no name-keyed carve-out, no "tcl mode" flag, and no widening of a construct only far enough to admit one vendor's header. The failing constructs must be identified as what they ARE in the C grammar (e.g. a declarator form, an attribute placement, a macro-expansion shape) and implemented for the whole language, so an ARBITRARY third-party header — not just Tcl's and zlib's — parses by construction. The correct witness is therefore NOT "tcl.h now parses": it is a strict test at the construct's own tier plus at least one header from a DIFFERENT library exercising the same construct, so the fix is proven general rather than fitted. | `D-TARGET-ENCODING-WIDTH-GUARD` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — KEEP P1. LD-3 LANDED 2026-09-03 (cycle P58, lane `ew`): `long double` COMPARISON, on BOTH axes. ⚠⚠ BUT DO NOT RE-BAND THIS ROW DOWN — I WENT LOOKING FOR PERMISSION TO AND THE MEASUREMENT REFUSED IT.** ✔MEASURED BEFORE, by this lane independently, running the UNMODIFIED main-tree `build/dbg` compiler at 01642ee3 over `examples/c/c_long_double_compare/main.c`: rc **1** on `x86_64:elf64-x86_64-linux-exec` (ordinal 14) and rc **1** on `arm64:elf64-aarch64-linux-exec` (ordinal 15). ⚠ **THAT BASELINE REFUTED THE ROW'S OWN SCOPE SENTENCE, WHICH SAID "THE `MIR FCmp operand` ARM" IN THE SINGULAR.** The gate reaches an FCmp from TWO places and BOTH fired: `MIR FCmp operand` from `lowerFCmp`, and `MIR FCmp operand (fused)` from `lowerCondBr`'s fused float-branch arm — which is the arm `if (a < b)` takes, the commonest shape in real code. Un-walling only the first would have been a partial fix that read as a complete one, so the F80 realization went into `emitFloatCompare`, the ONE shared flag-producing verb both callers already route through. **AS-BUILT, two realizations and one tail:** (a) **F80 / x87-80 axis** — the inline sequence `fld_m80 [rhs]; fld_m80 [lhs]; fucomip; fstp_st0`, from two NEW bare-2-byte opcode rows in `x86_64.target.json` (`fucomip` = DF E9, `fstp_st0` = DD D8, both byte-verified against `as`). FUCOMIP reports into **EFLAGS** with UCOMISD's ZF/PF/CF pattern bit for bit, so `floatCmpPlan`'s derivation, its Olt/Ole swap canonicalization, its Foeq/Fune two-setcc compositions and the fused jcc ALL apply with nothing re-derived — a drop-in for the `fcmp` emit, not a parallel path. ★ THE PUSH ORDER IS THE REVERSE of `lowerF80Arith`'s (FUCOMIP compares ST(0) against ST(1), so the LEFT operand is pushed LAST); inverting it flips every relational operator silently, and `fstp_st0` pops the survivor so the sequence still starts and ends with an EMPTY x87 stack. Quiet FUCOMIP, not the signaling FCOMIP both references emit for `< <= > >=`, because the `fcmp` row already chose quiet UCOMISD for every F64/F32 predicate on the stated ground that DSS models no FP environment — identical results, only the unobservable FE_INVALID differs. (b) **F128 / ieee128 axis** — a CALL to one of SIX new config'd `wideFloatSoftcalls[]` rows in `arm64.target.json`, then a **width-32** integer compare of the helper's `int` result against zero, finishing on the SIGNED integer nibble. ⚠⚠ **THE WIDTH-32 IS THE ONE SILENT-MISCOMPILE RISK ON THAT AXIS**: AAPCS64 leaves the upper 32 bits of the result register UNSPECIFIED for a 32-bit return, so a width-64 compare could take the predicate's sign from garbage. ✔Proven at the BYTE level in the emitted binary — `cmp w0, #0x0` encodes `7100001f`, the 32-bit form (the 64-bit `f100001f` appears two instructions later on the Bool). Gated on the PRESENCE of the row, never target/format identity: a target declaring F128 with no comparison rows falls through to this gate and fails LOUD. ⚠ **SECOND REFUTATION — THE CLOSING WORK NAMED `__letf2`/`__getf2`, WHICH IS TWO OF SIX.** ✔MEASURED, both aarch64 references probed SEPARATELY and agreeing helper-for-helper (aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc 13.3.0 `bl __lttf2; cmp w0,0; blt`; clang 18.1.3 `bl __lttf2; cset w0, lt`): C's six comparison operators map ONE-TO-ONE onto `__lttf2 __letf2 __gttf2 __getf2 __eqtf2 __netf2`, each returning the sign that makes ordered predicates FALSE and `!=` TRUE on a NaN operand — exactly C 6.5.9. Shipping only the two named would have left `<`, `>`, `==` and `!=` walled. ⓘ Deliberately NO row for libgcc's three-way `__cmptf2`: unspecified on NaN, and `nm -D /usr/aarch64-linux-gnu/lib/libgcc_s.so.1` shows it is not even EXPORTED, while all six of these are (which matters because DSS EAGER-IMPORTS every symbol a descriptor lists). ⚠ **THIRD REFUTATION — THE AXIS LIST IS WIDER THAN THE ROW IMPLIES**: `macho64-x86_64-darwin-*` also declares `longDoubleFormat: x87-80`, so the F80 arm serves macOS-x86_64 too, not only ELF. **WITNESS** — `examples/c/c_long_double_compare`, 16 arms, exit 42, each naming the property it measures because a long-double program that returns 42 proves NO FAULT and nothing about whether the comparison was CORRECT: arms 1-6 DIRECTION (every relational operator over an asymmetric pair BOTH ways — a push-order inversion flips all six), 7-9 the EQUALITY BOUNDARY (where a wrong condition-code nibble diverges from a merely wrong direction), 10-13 `==`/`!=` both ways (the composed two-setcc tail), 14-16 WIDTH — a comparison whose operands differ in their first few mantissa bits survives narrowing to `double` and witnesses nothing, so these discover the ULP OF 1.0 at runtime by comparison (self-calibrating: 2^-52 on the f64 axis, 2^-63 on x87, 2^-112 on binary128, no `<float.h>` needed since DSS ships none) and arm 16 breaks their circularity — a UNIFORM narrowing would let 14-15 pass by stopping the loop early — by CROSS-CHECKING the comparison against long-double ARITHMETIC, which is full-width by construction. ⓘ Arm 14's loop is ALSO the x87 STACK-BALANCE arm: its condition is a long-double compare evaluated once per mantissa bit, the stack is eight deep and each compare pushes two, so leaking either pop overflows it by the eighth iteration — a straight-line witness never reaches that. ✔RUNS to 42 on all three axes × debug AND release: `elf64-x86_64-linux-exec` native WSL, `elf64-aarch64-linux-exec` under qemu-aarch64 (DT_NEEDED libgcc_s.so.1, all six helpers imported UND), `pe64-x86_64-windows-exec` native Windows. ✔REFERENCE ORACLE, each probed SEPARATELY, all 42 at -O0 AND -O2: WSL gcc 13.3.0, WSL clang 18.1.3, mingw-w64 gcc 13.2.0 native, aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc 13.3.0 under qemu; MSVC 19.44 compiles it clean at `/std:c17` (an ABSTENTION on the 80-bit width, a positive vote on the construct). **PINS** — six in `tests/lir/test_mir_to_lir.cpp` (`MirToLir.F80CompareLowersToX87SequenceAndPushesTheLeftOperandLast`, `…F80CompareSwapCanonicalizationReversesThePushOrder`, `…F80CompareFusesIntoTheConditionalBranch`, `…F128CompareLowersToSoftcallAndWidth32IntegerCompare`, `…F128CompareFailsLoudWithoutConfigRow`, `…F128CompareFeedingABranchCallsTheHelperExactlyOnce`); all three direction arms pin the push order ABSOLUTELY against the `arg` opcodes' own result registers, because a relative pin is satisfied by a globally reversed order. Both are C++ selectany/RTTI/vtable constructs essentially absent from C/SQLite. NOTE: an ASSOCIATIVE COMDAT on a kind-UNRESOLVED metadata section (a `/Gy` object's `.pdata`/`.xdata`, cl.exe-witnessed selection 5) is NOT this case -- it is skipped whole by Gate 2 (`D-LK-COFF-FOREIGN-UNWIND-DROP`), never reconstructs a body, so its selection is never read (Gate 3 is gated on `sec.kind.has_value()`). Trigger: static-linking a real C++ `.obj`/`.lib` whose vtable/RTTI/selectany data lands in a LARGEST or ASSOCIATIVE code/data COMDAT. Closing work: size-aware selection resolution that stays format-BLIND -- a reader-side pre-resolution (pick the largest / associated body before the merge module is minted) or a size tiebreak that never touches the pure cross_cu_resolve kernel. | Size-aware LARGEST/ASSOCIATIVE COMDAT selection (reader-side pre-resolution or a format-blind size tiebreak). | `D-LK-COFF-READER-FOREIGN-OBJECT` (TF-C53, the parent); `src/link/format/coff_object_reader.cpp` (Gate 3) |
| `D-LK-COFF-FOREIGN-UNWIND-DROP` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | ⏳ **OPEN -- follow-up (named 2026-07-22, TF-C53).** The TF-C53 COFF reader SKIPS every kind-UNRESOLVED metadata section (`.pdata`/`.xdata`/`.debug$S`/`.debug$T`/`.drectve`/`.chks64`) AND its relocations (Gate 2) -- DSS's AssembledModule has no representation for SEH / x64 unwind, CodeView, or linker directives. So a foreign function's `.pdata`/`.xdata` unwind info is DROPPED: fine for a leaf / exit-42 link + for functions never unwound through, but a real correctness limitation for a foreign function that throws / is unwound (a stack-walking runtime cannot find its unwind info). The skip is gated on kind-UNRESOLVED, NOT the DISCARDABLE bit (`.pdata`/`.xdata` are 0x40000040, NOT discardable -- cl.exe-witnessed), so it fires whether or not the section is an ASSOCIATIVE COMDAT (a `/Gy` object marks them associative). Trigger: static-linking a real `.obj` whose functions require unwind (C++ EH, SEH, or a stack-walking runtime). Closing work: reconstruct `.pdata`/`.xdata` into a modeled unwind representation the PE EXEC writer re-emits (the RUNTIME_FUNCTION + UNWIND_INFO round-trip). | Reconstruct + re-emit foreign `.pdata`/`.xdata` SEH/unwind (currently skipped). | `D-LK-COFF-READER-FOREIGN-OBJECT` (TF-C53, Gate 2); `src/link/format/coff_object_reader.cpp` |

A PC-relative data reference whose base is a DIFFERENT slot than the atom it lives in (a hypothetical multi-atom PIC data section where entries relocate against a base not equal to their own atom's start) would mis-compute the search offset. gcc does NOT emit this for the string/switch constructs in c167's scope; a mis-land FAILS LOUD (`F_CorruptedBinary` -- lands in no reconstructed atom) rather than silently corrupting. Trigger: a real member with a multi-atom PC-relative data section whose reference base != atom base. Closing work: carry the reference base explicitly (or detect the multi-atom case) instead of assuming atom-base. | Handle a PC-relative data reference whose base != its containing atom's base (currently fails loud). | `D-LK-STATIC-LINK-SECTION-RELATIVE-RELOC` (c167); `src/link/format/elf_object_reader.cpp` (the §7 searchOff) |

Today EVERY DSS exec target is 64-bit (x86_64-PE/ELF, arm64-ELF/Mach-O) and LP64 + LLP64 agree on pointer width (8 bytes), so the pointer-width + greatest-width typedefs in the neutral `stdint.json`/`stddef.json` (`intptr_t`/`uintptr_t`/`intmax_t`/`uintmax_t`/`size_t`/`ptrdiff_t`) are correctly i64/u64. A single literal hir-text typedef can't express "pointer-width" across both ABIs, so the close is a per-target width resolution: the descriptor declares the typedef as a width-CLASS the semantic phase resolves against the active target's `dataModel`/pointer width (NOT an `if (target == i386)` in the engine — the descriptor/loader declares the width axis, the engine reads it). Pin: an ILP32 target compiles a program asserting `sizeof(intptr_t)==4` while a 64-bit target sees 8. | cited in `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/{stdint,stddef}.json` ($comment); pairs with `D-LANG-PLATFORM-DEPENDENT-PRIMITIVE-WIDTH` + `D-FFI-WCHAR-WIDTH` (both "a descriptor baked an ABI/platform width") + `D-FFI-STDINT-LIMIT-MACROS` (the width-dependent *_MAX inherit this); SQLite-readiness Cluster G (p18) |

| `D-SEMANTIC-MAYBE-UNINITIALIZED-WARN` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — the gate has FIRED and this row is SCHEDULABLE NOW: opened 2026-07-07 by [[D-OPT-MEM2REG-CONDITIONAL-INIT-UNDEF]], whose status cell reads CLOSED as of 2026-09-03.** Removing the Mem2Reg abort on conditional-init (a compiler must compile valid C) dropped the incidental "genuine uninitialized read" signal it provided. Trigger: a maybe-uninitialized diagnostic is prioritized (or a corpus wants it). Resolution: a semantic-tier maybe-uninitialized warning. | `D-CSUBSET-COMPOSITE-ALIGNED-HIR-TEXT` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — the gate has FIRED and this row is SCHEDULABLE NOW: opened 2026-07-28 by [[D-CSUBSET-COMPOSITE-ALIGNED]], whose status cell reads CLOSED as of 2026-09-03.** The HIR TEXT type round-trip (`src/hir/hir_text.cpp`) serializes a composite's `packed` flag (an `internPacked` key-space) and its per-field `~align` / `@offset` channels, but has NO syntax for the WHOLE-COMPOSITE alignment — so a `struct S {…} __attribute__((aligned(16)))` written and re-read through HIR text comes back with `explicitCompositeAlign == 0`, silently UNDER-aligned and (for the packed witness) sized 5 instead of 16. NOT a workaround was applied: the writer/parser live in a file outside this cycle's ownership. NOTE the in-memory cross-CU merge path (`reinternType`) DOES carry the channel and is pinned (`TypeReintern.CompositeExplicitAlignSurvivesReintern`), so this affects only text serialization. TRIGGER: any pipeline stage that round-trips composite types through HIR text, or a golden that must reproduce an aligned composite. Resolution: add a composite-level alignment token to the `struct`/`union` type syntax in `hir_text.cpp` (mirroring the existing `packed` keyword), thread it into the `internPacked`/`structType` calls, and extend the forward-key mix so an aligned and an unaligned same-name composite do not collide; pin with a text round-trip asserting `explicitCompositeAlign` + the resulting `sizeof`. | Add composite-alignment syntax to the HIR text type grammar + thread it through `hir_text.cpp`'s intern calls and forward key | opened by [[D-CSUBSET-COMPOSITE-ALIGNED]] (TF-C73); `src/hir/hir_text.cpp`, `tests/hir/test_hir_text.cpp` |
| `D-CSUBSET-NORETURN-VALUE-POSITION` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — the gate has FIRED and this row is SCHEDULABLE NOW: opened 2026-07-08 by [[D-CSUBSET-NORETURN]], whose status cell reads CLOSED as of 2026-09-03.** The noreturn-call HIR wrap fires ONLY at the `lowerStmtExprCore(wrapBare=true)` STATEMENT chokepoint (block-item / bare if/while/for/label arm). A noreturn call appearing in a NON-statement position — a for-INIT/UPDATE clause (`wrapBare=false`, deliberately untouched), or nested inside a value expression / cast / comma operator (`(void)die(1)`, `x, die(1)`) — is NOT wrapped, so a non-void function whose ONLY terminating path routes through such a form keeps a SPURIOUS H_VerifierFailure. FAIL-LOUD (a loud over-rejection), NEVER a silent miscompile — and vanishingly rare (a noreturn call is idiomatically a bare statement). TRIGGER: a real consumer terminates a function via a noreturn call in value/for-clause position. Resolution: extend the wrap to the value-expression lowering (an ExprStmt-free Unreachable emission) + the for-clause caller, with the SAME F1 direct-Ref-callee guard. | (gated — no shipped consumer needs a value-position noreturn call; the bare-statement form covers the idiom) | opened by the noreturn cycle; `src/hir/lowering/cst_to_hir.cpp` (`lowerStmtExprCore` / `lowerForClause`) |
| `D-MIR-STRUCTCF-DERIVATION-REACHES-PAST-THE-FUNCTION` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN (opened 2026-08-18, Cycle P9, by the markers lane that had to reproduce it bit-for-bit) — the struct-CF derivation reaches PAST the function being derived, and the reach is OBSERVABLE.** `mirDominatesBlock(s, u, dom)` answers `Dominates` whenever `s.v == u.v`, BEFORE consulting the tree — so a SELF-LOOPING block is its own back-edge source even in a function whose dominator tree never saw it. Rule 3 therefore claims a single-block pseudo-loop for EVERY self-looping block in the MODULE, in EVERY function's derivation, and claims `LoopExit` on those blocks' non-self successors. Two consequences: (i) an UNREACHABLE block can derive non-`Linear`, contradicting what the header's intent text used to say — reconciled this cycle by documenting the behaviour as canon and pinning it ([[D-MIR-STRUCTCF-UNREACHABLE-BLOCK-CLAIMED]]); (ii) deriving function F writes non-`Linear` markers into slots OWNED by function G — nothing in-tree consumes them (`applyDerived` reads only F's own slots; the verifier reads only F's REACHABLE blocks), but the returned vector carries them. NOT a defect today: it predates the O(function) rework, and the scoped back-edge sweep ([[D-OPT-NATURAL-LOOPS-MODULE-WIDE-SCAN]]) reproduces it ON PURPOSE — `ForeignSelfLoopPseudoLoopClaimIsPreserved` is RED if the reach is narrowed by accident. The reachable-only assumption is NOT load-bearing for soundness, though: the **fail-loud abort is the actual backstop** — if a future input ever carried an unreachable block, the merge aborts with attribution rather than emitting a malformed function, so it is never a silent miscompile. Trigger = the first merge input carrying an unreachable block (e.g. a future frontend that emits an entry-unreachable block, or a debug/identity pipeline that runs no DCE on input that happens to contain one). | `D-PERF-WINDOWS-HOST-COMPILES-8X-SLOWER-THAN-LINUX` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — created 2026-08-18 (Cycle P8) BY OPERATOR INSTRUCTION, to be triaged with the post-commit anchor pass.** ★★★ **UPDATED 2026-08-18 (Cycle P9): THE 8× PREMISE WAS MEASURED WRONG — the comparison timed a DEBUG compiler on Windows against RELEASE compilers on every POSIX leg.** `build-and-test.ps1`'s `Find-Dss` picked the newest binary regardless of build type (this box: `build/dbg`, `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug`, -O0) while the `.sh` twin builds Release unconditionally, and neither log said which — closed as [[D-HARNESS-PS1-TIMES-A-DEBUG-COMPILER-WHILE-THE-SH-TWIN-TIMES-A-RELEASE-ONE]]. ✔RE-MEASURED with the variable controlled — both compilers Release, built from this tree, 103-TU sqlite CLI compile, target `x86_64:elf64-x86_64-linux-exec`; ⚠ the four-leg figures are the P9 profile taken with the variable controlled but BEFORE P9's engine fixes landed (they are the honest BEFORE record, not a post-fix claim): **Windows 3m32.1s · WSL 1m40.8s (same machine) ⇒ real residual ~2.1×, not 8×**; arm64 VPS 3m18.1s, macOS 5m10.0s; gcc yardstick on WSL 21.5s `-j1` / 4.8s `-j32`. ★★★ **POST-P9-FIX UPDATE (same kit, same target, ✔MEASURED at the acceptance run): Windows median 40.7 s (20 repeats, 38.6–45.4 s) · WSL 33.6 s — the pre-fix ~2.1× Windows-vs-WSL residual COLLAPSED to ~1.2×, because the quadratic rederive and the module-sized allocation churn were exactly the work whose cost Windows' allocator/IO amplified.** The row stays OPEN against the residual ~1.2× and the four candidate causes (a)–(d) below, none yet separated — but any future experiment must quote the POST-FIX numbers; the 18-minute and 2.1× figures are the record of the Debug artefact and the pre-fix engine respectively. Candidates (a)–(d) below REMAIN UNSEPARATED — the row stays OPEN against the ~2.1× residual, and the original 18m/2m figures are retained below as the record of what the -O0 artifact looked like. **ORIGINAL FINDING: THE WINDOWS HOST COMPILES THE SAME SOURCE SET ~8× SLOWER THAN WSL ON THE SAME MACHINE, AND ~3.6× SLOWER THAN A SMALL ARM VPS.** ✔MEASURED 2026-08-18, one sqlite `veryquick`/`release` harness run per host, SAME upstream sqlite stage, SAME ~185-TU full-source testfixture, SAME shipped `release` pipeline, all four hosts on the same day and the same compiler tree:   **Windows host** (MinGW-w64 13.2 / libstdc++, 61.7 GB RAM): elf64-x86_64 **18m59.202s** · elf64-arm64 **18m26.565s** · pe64-x86_64 **18m09.741s** · macho64-arm64 **16m24.955s**   **WSL x86_64** (same physical machine): **2m16.105s** · **2m20.486s** · **2m28.038s** · **2m21.571s** · **2m20.738s**   **arm64 VPS** (a modest cloud instance, NOT this desktop): **4m55.547s** · **4m57.073s** · **5m19.785s** · **5m00.155s** · **4m58.512s** ★★ **THE COMPARISON THAT MAKES THIS WORTH A ROW IS NOT WINDOWS-vs-WSL, IT IS WINDOWS-vs-THE-VPS.** WSL runs on the SAME hardware, so an 8× gap there is already an indictment of something in the Windows path rather than of the machine — but the arm64 VPS is a SEPARATE, SMALLER box and still finishes in **~5 minutes against ~18**. A 61.7 GB desktop losing 3.6× to a small cloud ARM instance is not explainable by hardware. ✔ALSO MEASURED, and it is what makes a resource cause plausible: the compile runs as ONE `dss-code-prime.exe` process reaching **8.7–9.2 GB** resident, single-threaded, ~1,219 s of CPU on one leg — i.e. the time is really being SPENT, not spent waiting on a lock. Free RAM never dropped below ~32 GB, so this is not swapping. ⚠⚠ **NO CAUSE IS CLAIMED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** The candidates have NOT been separated: (a) real-time antivirus over a 9 GB working set and a large temp tree; (b) Windows page-fault / heap-growth behaviour under a single huge arena where glibc's allocator is cheaper; (c) filesystem cost (NTFS + `/mnt/c` vs ext4) on the many small reads a 185-TU project does; (d) a genuine codegen or IO cost on the MinGW build of the compiler itself. **Naming one of these without an experiment is exactly the guess this registry exists to prevent.** ★ IT IS NOT A CORRECTNESS DEFECT — all four Windows legs built and the artifacts are green. It is a DEVELOPER-THROUGHPUT defect, and the reason it earns a row rather than a shrug is that the Windows host is the operator's PRIMARY box and the local gate is run there many times a day. | **Trigger: FIRED** — measured, reproducible across four legs today. **Closing work, and step one is an EXPERIMENT, not an edit.** (1) SEPARATE THE CANDIDATES, one variable at a time, each on the SAME leg for comparability: run one leg with Defender exclusions on the repo + `%TEMP%` + the build tree; run the same leg from an ext4-backed path inside WSL using the WINDOWS-built compiler under Wine if that is cheaper than the alternative, or failing that compare `/mnt/c` vs a native WSL path with the LINUX compiler to isolate filesystem cost; profile one compile with a sampling profiler to see whether the time is in the allocator, in IO, or in a pass. (2) Only then decide whether the fix is environmental (an exclusion, documented in the build instructions), structural (arena/allocator behaviour on Windows), or a pass-level cost that shows up on both hosts and is merely amplified here. ⚠ **DO NOT "FIX" THIS BY MEASURING A DIFFERENT WORKLOAD.** The number that matters is the one the operator actually waits for: a full-source `--project` build, not a synthetic microbenchmark. ★ Closing evidence: the same four legs re-measured on the same host with the cause named and the remedy applied, quoted beside these baselines. | `real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.ps1` (the harness that produced the numbers) · [[D-BUILD-OUT-OF-TREE-WINDOWS-MAX-PATH-FRAGILE]] (the other Windows-host-only build defect) · [[reference_windows_build_dirs_bigobj_2026_07_22]] |
| `D-CSUBSET-APPLE-SDK-HEADER-SURFACE` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — priority normal, trigger ALREADY FIRED (first macOS-arm64 sqlite corpus run, 2026-07-27). Error profile moved from "header not found" to "cannot parse the header": **153 errors = 50 × P0015 + 49 × P0009 + 47 × P0001 + 7 × F001A** _(⚠ **STALE** as of 2026-07-27 — SUPERSEDED by the RE-MEASUREMENT below, which also re-attributes gaps (1)–(4); read the gap list that follows as the ORIGINAL attribution, not the current one)_. ★ THE FOUR CONCRETE GAPS (measured from `compile.log`, by construct): **(1) `#error` — 50 hits, `P0015 unsupported preprocessor directive (out of FC13 cycle-1 scope): error`.** The single biggest count and the cheapest to close; C 6.10.5, and SDK headers use it as the standard "unsupported configuration" guard. NOTE it must be implemented with correct `#if` awareness — an `#error` in a NOT-TAKEN branch must stay silent, so this is not merely "recognise the directive". **(2) `__attribute__((…))` — the `P0001 expected 'EndStatement' — got '__attribute__'` / `got 'aligned'` family.** The GNU/clang attribute syntax, pervasive in `sys/cdefs.h` (32 errors) which nearly every Apple header includes. **(3) `__asm("name")` symbol-label syntax — 8 × `P0009 got '__asm'`.** Darwin's `sys/cdefs.h` renames symbols via `__asm` labels (the `$UNIX2003`/`$INODE64` suffix mechanism); it also explains the 8 × `got '"'`. **(4) a few still-unresolved headers (7 × F001A):** `stdbool.h` (DSS ships no descriptor and the SDK's own is a compiler-supplied header), `malloc/_malloc_type.h`, and the `mach/*` cluster — `mach_types.h`, `kern_return.h`, `port.h`, `vm_types.h`, `boolean.h`. **BY FILE:** TargetConditionals.h 41, sys/cdefs.h 32, malloc/malloc.h 26, bsm/audit.h 18, sys/_types/_fd_def.h 8, machine/*.h 4, + sqlite's own os_unix.c 10 / mem1.c 9 / test1.c 2 (the Darwin arms themselves). ★★ **RE-MEASURED 2026-07-27 (TF-C72, post-TF-C71, from `build/real-examples/c/sqlite/host/compile.log`) — the 153-error profile above is SUPERSEDED; every count in (1)–(4) is re-attributed here. MEASURED: 239 errors = 136 × `P001E` + 49 × `P0009` + 47 × `P0001` + 7 × `F001A`; plus 44 × `info[P000D]` and 3 × `warning[P001F]`.** ★ **CORRECTION 1 — the `#error` class is 136, not the 49 × `P001E` this row predicted, and it is NOT this row's gap at all.** They are not scattered `#error`s: **112** are the SINGLE message `TargetConditionals.h: unknown compiler (see comment above)`, **21** are `architecture not supported`, **3** are `Unsupported architecture`. Every one of the 136 is a compiler/arch **IDENTITY gate** — the header refusing to admit DSS exists, not a C construct the front-end cannot parse — so NONE of the 136 belongs to this row's gap list. ⚠ **The onward attribution in the next clause — “the whole 136 belongs to [[D-CSUBSET-DARWIN-PLATFORM-MACROS]]” — is SUPERSEDED and was WRONG for 112 of the 136: see ★ CORRECTION 5 below, which SPLITS the 136 into a TOOLCHAIN-identity class and an ARCHITECTURE class and is the CURRENT attribution. Architecture predefines alone clear 24, not 136.** (Original, superseded wording retained for the audit trail: the whole 136 belongs to [[D-CSUBSET-DARWIN-PLATFORM-MACROS]], NOT to this row's gap list.) (Gap (1), the DIRECTIVE, is separately ✅ closed by [[D-CPP-ERROR-WARNING]]/TF-C70; implementing it converted a parse gap into a platform-identity gap, which is why the count moved rather than vanished.) ★ **CORRECTION 2 — gap (2)'s "47 hits" is 47 DIAGNOSTICS, not 47 SITES.** One `__attribute__` emits ~4 as the parser resyncs, so the whole 47 comes from only **SIX distinct source constructs**; and of the 47, only ~**26** are attribute — the other ~**21** are `__asm`-label expansion, i.e. gap (3) bleeding into gap (2)'s column. MEASURED gap (3) total: **21 × `P0001` + 16 × `P0009`**. Sizing gap (2) off the raw diagnostic count over-states it by ~8×. ★ **CORRECTION 3 (NEW ATTRIBUTION, MEASURED) — gap (4), the "7-header tail", is worth ~25 MORE errors than its `7 × F001A` reads: ~25 of the 49 `P0009` are a CASCADE from the unresolved `malloc/_malloc_type.h`** — with that header missing, `_MALLOC_TYPED(...)` is an undefined macro, so `malloc/malloc.h` cannot parse. Resolving ONE header collapses ~25 `P0009`; far more leverage than the ordering above ("the residual tail") suggests. ★ **CORRECTION 4 (NEW CROSS-LINK, MEASURED) — the 8 × `P0009` `got 'void'` / `got 'int'` that follow `__header_always_inline` are the SAME `inline` function-specifier defect already filed as gap (1) of [[D-CSUBSET-TCL9-HEADER-CONSTRUCTS]]** (`static inline void`, Tcl 9 `tcl.h:2544`). ONE defect, TWO headers, two anchors that did not previously link — fixing the `inline` specifier pays out in both, and whoever closes it must re-measure BOTH rows. The cross-link is recorded in BOTH rows. ★ **That defect now has its OWN owning row, [[D-CSUBSET-INLINE-FUNCTION-SPECIFIER]] (created 2026-07-28) — go there for the full measurement; this row and [[D-CSUBSET-TCL9-HEADER-CONSTRUCTS]] are now its two CONSUMERS, not its owners.** ★★ **CORRECTION 5 (2026-07-28, macOS-arm64 predefine-probe cycle, post-TF-C73) — THE 136 ARE TWO DIFFERENT DEFECTS AND CORRECTION 1's SINGLE ATTRIBUTION IS REPLACED BY THIS SPLIT.** CORRECTION 1 was right that all 136 are identity gates and right that none is this row's parse gap, but it sent all 136 to [[D-CSUBSET-DARWIN-PLATFORM-MACROS]] (a PLATFORM row). MEASURED split of the 136 (RELAYED from the cycle's probe builds — not independently re-run when this correction was recorded): **112 × `TargetConditionals.h: unknown compiler`** at `TargetConditionals.h:391`, the `#else` of `#if defined(__GNUC__) && (defined(__APPLE_CPP__) \|\| defined(__APPLE_CC__) \|\| defined(__MACOS_CLASSIC__))` — that is **TOOLCHAIN identity, not architecture, and not platform**; **21 × `architecture not supported`**, the `machine/_types.h:33` shape (`#if defined(__i386__)\|\|defined(__x86_64__)` / `#elif defined(__arm__)\|\|defined(__arm64__)` / `#else #error`); and **3 × `Unsupported architecture`**, the 4-arm `#elif …defined(__arm64__)` ladder at `sys/cdefs.h:1063-1068`. ★ **CONSEQUENCE FOR SIZING: ARCHITECTURE predefines alone clear 24 of 136 (21 + 3), NOT 136** — the remaining 112 need TOOLCHAIN identity and are owned by the new [[D-CSUBSET-TOOLCHAIN-IDENTITY-PREDEFINES]]. See [[D-INCLUDE-ANGLE-SOURCE-FALLBACK]] (the resolution-order corollary) and [[D-FFI-DARWIN-TARGETCONDITIONALS-EMPTY]] (the correctness cost of the shadow). ═══ OPEN — trigger fired. Suggested order by leverage: `#error` (1 directive, 50 hits, must respect `#if` skipping) → `__attribute__` (unblocks sys/cdefs.h, hence nearly every Apple header) → `__asm` labels → the residual `mach/*`/`stdbool.h`/`_malloc_type.h` tail. Closing witness: `DSS_TCL_VERSION=8.6 DSS_TIER=veryquick bash real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.sh` compiles the 189-TU testfixture on macOS and the corpus RUNS (that run is what produces the first macOS corpus verdict — still UNOBTAINED as of this row). | ★ This is the strongest evidence yet for the [[D-FFI-SHIPPED-LIBS-OS-ONLY]] consequence noted in [[D-CSUBSET-TCL9-HEADER-CONSTRUCTS]]: now that DSS parses REAL vendor/OS headers rather than curated descriptors, the front-end is exposed to the full C dialect those headers are written in — GNU attributes, asm labels, `#error`. Expect this class to keep appearing on every new platform, and treat "can we parse the platform's own SDK headers?" as a first-class portability question for each new target. | [[D-CSUBSET-DARWIN-PLATFORM-MACROS]] (the fix that made these arms reachable); [[D-CSUBSET-TCL9-HEADER-CONSTRUCTS]] (the sibling real-header parse gap); [[D-INCLUDE-ANGLE-SOURCE-FALLBACK]] (the mechanism that parses these headers); [[D-FFI-SHIPPED-LIBS-OS-ONLY]] (why real headers are parsed at all); [[D-CPP-ERROR-WARNING]] (closed gap (1)); ★ the 2026-07-28 CORRECTION-5 successors [[D-CSUBSET-TOOLCHAIN-IDENTITY-PREDEFINES]] (owns 112 of the 136) · [[D-CSUBSET-INLINE-FUNCTION-SPECIFIER]] (owns CORRECTION 4's 8) · [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-LEADING-WITH-STORAGE-CLASS]] (the leading-position blocker that gates them); the TF-C72 attribute successors [[D-CSUBSET-GNU-ATTRIBUTE-LEADING-ARG-SOUP]] · [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-ARG-CONSTANT-EXPRESSION]] · [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-MEMBER-POSITION]]; `build/real-examples/c/sqlite/host/compile.log` · `MacOSX.sdk/usr/include/{TargetConditionals.h,sys/cdefs.h,malloc/malloc.h,malloc/_malloc_type.h,libkern/OSAtomicDeprecated.h}` ★★ **GAP (4)'s HEADER TAIL NOW HAS ITS OWN OWNING ROW: [[D-CSUBSET-STDARG-F001A]] (opened 2026-07-29, TF-C86) — this row becomes its CONSUMER, not its owner, exactly as happened with [[D-CSUBSET-INLINE-FUNCTION-SPECIFIER]] for CORRECTION 4. ⚠ And do NOT quote this row's `7 × F001A` as the current F001A population: that figure comes from the stale overwritten `compile.log` and is one of THREE disagreeing instruments — see that row.** |
| `D-DIAG-INCLUDE-LOCATION-ATTRIBUTION` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★★★ a DIAGNOSTIC-USABILITY class, created 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 anchor pass), measured in passing during [[D-CSUBSET-STDARG-F001A]]'s close. TRIGGER *ALREADY FIRED*.** ⭐ **THE SYMPTOM, MEASURED: `F001A` pointed at `mem1.c:69763` while `src/mem1.c` is `291` LINES.** ★ **That is not an off-by-a-bit. It is a line number 240× past the end of the named file — a location that cannot be opened, cannot be jumped to, and cannot be checked. A reader who trusts it goes looking in the wrong file entirely, because the file it names is a real file that really exists and really does not contain the error.** ⭐⭐ **THE MECHANISM — ✔RE-VERIFIED BY THIS PASS in the committed tree at `5093341`, and the naming is the trap.** The preprocessor builds ONE flattened synthesized buffer for the whole TU, and constructs it as `SourceBuffer::fromString(synthText, mainSource->name())` — ★★ **THE SYNTH BUFFER IS NAMED AFTER THE TU-ROOT SOURCE FILE.** So a diagnostic that retains synth-buffer coordinates does not render as `<synth>` or `<unknown>` — **it renders under the TU root's real filename with the flattened post-include line number, i.e. it LOOKS LIKE A VALID LOCATION AND IS NOT.** ★ **Had the synth buffer been named `<synth:mem1.c>`, this would have been obvious on sight for a year; the plausibility of the render is what made it survive.** ⭐ **WHY THE EXISTING REMAP DID NOT CATCH IT — AND THIS IS THE PART THAT DECIDES THE CROSS-LINK QUESTION.** `PreprocessResult::makeRemap()` exists and works: it self-gates on the synth buffer id and redirects a span onto its true origin buffer via the line map. ✔MEASURED, it is applied at exactly TWO places — (1) per-tree diagnostics (`tree.remapDiagnostics(ppRemap)`) and (2) the `shippedLibDescriptors` refs' carried `#include` span. **`F_ShippedHeaderNotFound` is emitted by neither of those tiers: `import_resolver.cpp` reports it via `reportDriver(context.diagnostics, …, sourceBuffer, directive.span, …)` — the CU's DRIVER tier — and `program.cpp` drains driver diagnostics straight into the run-wide reporter with NO remap applied.** ⚠ **INFERRED (strongly evidenced, NOT measured end-to-end by this pass): the driver tier is the un-remapped tier, and that is why this diagnostic renders in synth coordinates while a parser or semantic diagnostic in the same header does not. CONFIRM THIS FIRST — see closing work (A).** ⚠⚠ **PREMISE CORRECTION, RECORDED SO THE ROW IS NOT LARGER THAN ITS EVIDENCE: this row was minted on the claim that "EVERY diagnostic located inside an included header is currently unusable for navigation". ✔MEASURED, THAT IS TOO BROAD.** TREE-tier diagnostics ARE remapped, and the behaviour is PINNED by a live test (`test_preprocessor.cpp::IncludeSpliceDiagnosticsRenderToRealFilesAndLines`) which asserts a header error renders as `bad.h:1:` and a post-include main error as `main.c:2:`, with RED-on-disable demonstrated. **The real scope is: diagnostics emitted on tiers the remap was never wired to, of which `F_ShippedHeaderNotFound` is the measured instance. Scope the fix to the TIER, not to "all header diagnostics".** | ★★★ **CLOSING WORK.** ⭐ **(A) FIRST, ESTABLISH THE TIER BOUNDARY — do not fix before measuring it.** Enumerate every diagnostic-producing tier that can carry a synth-buffer span (driver / import-resolver / FFI-ingest / semantic / anything post-parse) and determine which are run through `ppRemap` and which are not. ★ **The answer is the size of this defect, and right now nobody knows it: this pass found ONE un-remapped tier by following ONE diagnostic. There is no inventory.** ⭐ **(B) FIX AT THE CHOKE POINT, NOT PER-CALL-SITE.** ⚠ **Remapping each newly-discovered emit site individually is how this recurs — it is the same shape as the anchor-registry leak that recurred TWICE before a guard made the system enforce it.** Apply the remap where diagnostics are COLLECTED (or make a synth-coordinate span un-renderable without passing through it), so a future tier added by a future cycle is covered by construction rather than by remembering. ⭐ **(C) MAKE THE FAILURE LOUD INSTEAD OF PLAUSIBLE — the cheapest and highest-value limb.** ★★ **A span whose line exceeds the named buffer's line count is a PROVABLE internal inconsistency and should be caught, not printed.** Assert or fail-loud in the renderer when a resolved line is past the end of the buffer it is being rendered against. **`mem1.c:69763` against a 291-line file is detectable with information the renderer already holds at the moment it renders.** ⚠ **Note the adjacency to [[D-DIAG-RENDERER-PAST-END-SPAN-HEAP-OVERREAD]] (a past-end span in the renderer was a real heap over-read) and to [[D-PP-REMAP-ORIGIN-OFFSET-UNVALIDATED]] — this is a THIRD symptom of the same untrusted-offset surface, and the three should be looked at together even though they close separately.** ⭐ **(D) CONSIDER RENAMING THE SYNTH BUFFER** to something that cannot be mistaken for a real path. ⚠ **Weigh it: the TU-root name is presentationally correct for the many diagnostics that ARE main-file-origin, so a rename trades one class of confusion for another and must not be done casually.** ⭐ **(E) RED-ON-DISABLE.** A TU whose angle-included system header is missing, asserting the diagnostic renders at the HEADER's own file and line — and that it does NOT render a line number past the end of whatever file it names. | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 anchor pass, at HEAD `5093341`). **PARENT:** [[D-CSUBSET-STDARG-F001A]] (measured during its close). ★★★ **CROSS-LINK, EXPLICITLY Tcl **8.6** headers use neither construct, which is why the three green legs (Windows-pe64, Linux-x86_64, Linux-arm64 — all on 8.6) never saw this. **THE TWO GAPS (both ordinary C the c-subset front-end does not yet accept):** **(1) the `inline` function-specifier** — `/opt/homebrew/Cellar/tcl-tk/9.0.3/include/tcl-tk/tcl.h:2544` is `static inline void` and the parser reports `expected 'Identifier', 'ParenOpen', 'StarOp', 'EndStatement', 'BracketOpen' or 'BlockOpen' — got 'void'`, i.e. `inline` is consumed as the declarator NAME and the real type head then surprises it. C99 6.7.4; pairs with `static`/`extern` on both declarations and definitions. ★ **CROSS-LINK ADDED 2026-07-27 (TF-C72), MEASURED — this is NOT a Tcl-9-only gap.** The SAME `inline` function-specifier defect produces the 8 × `P0009` `got 'void'` / `got 'int'` that follow Apple `sys/cdefs.h`'s `__header_always_inline`, counted under [[D-CSUBSET-APPLE-SDK-HEADER-SURFACE]]'s re-measured profile. ONE defect, TWO headers, two anchors that did not previously link; whoever closes gap (1) must re-measure BOTH rows, and the closing witness should include an Apple-header leg, not only a Tcl one. **(2) an ANONYMOUS STRUCT TYPE inside `sizeof`, whose body contains a `_Static_assert`** — `tclDecls.h:4046`, `#define TCLBOOLWARNING(boolPtr) (void)(sizeof(struct {_Static_assert(sizeof(*(boolPtr)) <= sizeof(int), "…");int dummy;})),` — the parser rejects `got 'void'` at the cast and `got 'struct'` at the `sizeof(struct {…})`, reported at `scope: Block > … > Paren > Paren > Paren`. Needs (a) `sizeof` over a TYPE-NAME that is an inline struct-definition (C 6.5.3.4 / 6.7.2.1), and (b) `_Static_assert` accepted as a struct-declaration member (C11 6.7.2.1p1 permits `static_assert-declaration` in a struct body). ★ ALSO OBSERVED, same run, first diagnostic of the file: `info[P000D] got schema cursor went off-track; contextual keyword resolution will stay strict for the remainder of the build` at `tcl.h:60:1` — determine whether that off-track latch is a CONSEQUENCE of gap (1)/(2) or an INDEPENDENT third defect before closing this row; if independent it gets its own anchor. **NOT A BLOCKER FOR THE CORPUS VERDICT:** `DSS_TCL_VERSION=8.6` (the pin added by the sibling anchor) puts macOS on the same Tcl major as the other three legs, which is how the first macOS corpus verdict is being obtained. This row is the record that Tcl 9 support is a REAL, already-triggered front-end gap — not a hypothetical. ═══ OPEN — trigger fired (Tcl 9 is Homebrew's default today, so any agnostic macOS run hits it). Closing work: implement the `inline` function-specifier and `sizeof(struct {…})` + `_Static_assert`-in-struct-body in the c-subset grammar/front-end, each with strict red-on-disable pins; then re-run the macOS corpus WITHOUT `DSS_TCL_VERSION` and confirm the 100 errors go to 0. Adjudicate the P000D off-track note first. | ★ The corpus exercise did exactly its job here: a version bump in a third-party dependency surfaced two genuine front-end gaps that no unit test covered, precisely because the descriptor deletion ([[D-FFI-SHIPPED-LIBS-OS-ONLY]], TF-C65) means DSS now PARSES the real vendor header instead of a curated descriptor — so the front-end is exposed to whatever C upstream actually writes. Expect more of this class as vendors modernise. **UPDATE 2026-07-31 (TF-C101) — TCL 9.0 NOW BUILDS, LINKS AND RUNS.** The user directive is to support the SYSTEM-DEFAULT Tcl, which on this host is **9.0** (`/opt/homebrew/opt/tcl-tk` -> `libtcl9.0.dylib`), NOT the 8.6 LTS. MEASURED before: the sqlite `testfixture` against Tcl 9.0 failed with **100 parse errors** (50 `P0009` + 50 `P0001`) from exactly TWO constructs — `__attribute__((__noreturn__))` leading a struct MEMBER declaration (`tcl.h:116` `TCL_NORETURN1`, used at `tclDecls.h:1893/:2024/:2185` on `TclStubs` function-pointer members) and `sizeof(struct { _Static_assert(...); int dummy; })` (`tclDecls.h:4046`). MEASURED after: **0 errors, links, and RUNS** — `otool -L` shows `/opt/homebrew/opt/tcl-tk/lib/libtcl9.0.dylib` (the system-default path) and the fixture reports SQLite 3.54.0 executing real SQL at exit 0. Both fixes are CONFIG-ONLY grammar changes; no engine change was needed for either. Remaining on this row: the `P000D` off-track adjudication.| [[D-HARNESS-TCL-VERSION-AGNOSTIC]] (the discovery change that surfaced it + the `DSS_TCL_VERSION` escape hatch); [[D-FFI-SHIPPED-LIBS-OS-ONLY]] (why real vendor headers are now parsed); [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-BSD-SED-PORTABILITY]] (the sibling macOS repair from the same run); `tcl.h:2544` · `tclDecls.h:4046` · `build/real-examples/c/sqlite/host/compile.log` |

A reader auditing the descriptor for completeness is told, in the file itself, that the completeness question has already been answered. A comment that supplies the rationale for what is ABSENT is load-bearing in the strongest sense available to a comment: it does not merely mislead, it terminates the search.** ⚠ **RULE: any `$comment` that justifies an OMISSION must cite the measurement that established it (command, date, corpus), or say plainly that it is an assumption. "X doesn't use this" is a claim about a moving target and must never be recorded as settled fact.** ⏳ **PRIOR UPDATE, unchanged below:** 🔴🔴 **★★★★ UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C85 CLOSE-OUT) — A *FOURTH* FALSE CLAIM, AND IT IS THE WORST OF THE FOUR. INSTANCES (a) AND (b) ARE REPAIRED; INSTANCE (c) IS STILL SHIPPING.** ✔MEASURED at TF-C85: **`src/dss-config/shippedLibs/intrin.json`'s `$comment` still asserts *"sqlite's byte-reverse fast path is #if'd out under DSS's config (`SQLITE_BYTEORDER!=1234` → the portable byte-by-byte `#else`) so nothing references them."* IT IS FALSE UNDER THE `pe` PROFILE: `SQLITE_BYTEORDER` IS 1234, THE FAST PATH IS LIVE, and `error[S0001]` fires at THREE real call sites — `btree.c:23784` (`_byteswap_ushort`), `util.c:24880` and `:24893` (`_byteswap_ulong`).** ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **WHAT MAKES THIS ONE STRICTLY WORSE THAN THE OTHER THREE, AND IT IS A DISTINCTION THIS CLASS DID NOT PREVIOUSLY DRAW: (a), (b1) and (b2) are STALE FACTS — sentences that were true and stopped being true. (c) IS A *LOAD-BEARING JUSTIFICATION FOR A DECISION*. It is the STATED REASON the whole `_byteswap_*` family IS NOT DECLARED AT ALL.** ★★★ **So the failure is not "a reader is misinformed"; it is "a DESIGN DECISION rests on a premise that is false, and the decision goes on looking justified after the premise dies". A stale fact rots. A stale JUSTIFICATION holds a gap open** — and it held this one open until an unrelated cycle happened to remove the pragma wall that was hiding the consumers. **ADD THIS TO THE CLASS'S DEFINITION: rank a `$comment` claim by WHAT RESTS ON IT, not by how wrong it is. A claim that merely describes costs a reader a re-check; a claim that JUSTIFIES costs the project the decision.** ⚠⚠ **AND THE DETECTION RECORD GETS WORSE IN THE SAME DIRECTION: this fourth instance was found by the SAME mechanism as the first three — an unrelated cycle reading the file for another reason.** Four for four. **Four instances, zero found on purpose, is no longer anecdote: the discovery rate for this defect class is a function of luck, and the closing work below has to produce a MECHANISM or say out loud that none exists.** ⭐ **STATUS OF THE REPAIRS, so the ✅ is not over-read: TF-C85 DID fix (a) — the `$tfc82PragmaComment` now names its instrument AND its profile, states the pe-class result, and explicitly calls its own earlier closed-world sentence FALSE — and DID fix (b1) and (b2) in `intrin.json`. It found (c) IN THE SAME FILE, in the SAME `$comment`, and only REPORTED it. So `intrin.json` currently carries a corrected paragraph and a false one side by side**, which is the most misleading possible state for a reader who sees the ★ correction notice and concludes the file has been swept. ★ **REPAIR OWED (small, and it must not be deferred behind the feature): re-state (c) as what is actually true — the `_byteswap_*` family is undeclared, the `pe` profile DOES reach real calls, `S0001` fires there BY DESIGN, and [[D-CSUBSET-INTRINSIC-BSWAP]] is now a FIRED trigger rather than a gated one.** **── AS FIRST FILED (kept verbatim; its "TWO false claims" framing is superseded by the four above):** 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a CONFIG-PROSE / CLAIM-DURABILITY CLASS, created 2026-07-29 (TF-C85 anchor pass). Filed as the CLASS the brief called `D-CONFIG-COMMENT-CLAIMS-GO-STALE`; named to match its sibling [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]].** ★★★ **TF-C85 CORRECTS *TWO* FALSE CLAIMS THAT ARE SHIPPING IN CONFIG RIGHT NOW, AND THEY ARE THE SAME SPECIES — WHICH IS WHY THIS IS ONE CLASS ROW AND NOT TWO FOOTNOTES.** **(a) `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json` `$tfc82PragmaComment`** — ✔TREE-VERIFIED at `7db3435` (`:92`): *"The REACHED census (`clang -E` over all 189 TUs with the recipe's real defines/includes) is: `clang diagnostic` 48, `pack` 40, `clang assume_nonnull` 24 -- and **NOTHING else**."* **MEASURED UNDER ONE PROFILE, WRITTEN AS UNIVERSAL.** The `pe` profile defines `_MSC_VER`; the instrument (`clang -E` on macOS) structurally could not, so an entire arm of the corpus's preprocessor state was invisible to the sentence that claims to have surveyed all of it — ⟲2135 `P0020` on 113 of 189 TUs is what "nothing else" was actually covering. **(b) `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/intrin.json` `$comment`** — ✔TREE-VERIFIED at `7db3435` (`:2`): *"DSS drops the pragma (unknown directive, C 6.10.6p2) and resolves the include here, so the amalgamation compiles WITHOUT -DSQLITE_DISABLE_INTRINSIC."* **UNTRUE SINCE TF-C82** (`3419ce9` made recognized-but-unmatched pragmas LOUD via `unknownPragmaIsError`), **and it names the EXACT pragma that now breaks `pe64`** — `#pragma intrinsic(...)`, ⟲448 of the 2135. ⚠ **The same sentence carries a SECOND, INDEPENDENT scope error: it justifies itself by "so the amalgamation compiles…", but ✔MEASURED, the corpus does not build the amalgamation — the harness uses `make -n testfixture USE_AMALGAMATION=0` (`real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.sh:21`, `.ps1:397`/`:400`) and the 189 sources in `build/real-examples/c/sqlite/host/host.dss-project.json` contain **NO `sqlite3.c` and NO `shell.c`**. So the comment's stated BENEFIT is a claim about a build this project does not perform.** ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **WHY THIS IS A CLASS AND NOT HOUSEKEEPING: THESE `$comment` BLOCKS ARE THOUSANDS OF WORDS OF JUSTIFICATION PROSE, AND THEY ARE LOAD-BEARING BY THE PROJECT'S OWN CONVENTION — a row asserts a CHECKABLE CLAIM in config the user reads.** ★★★ **A STALE ONE IS *WORSE* THAN A MISSING ROW. The missing row fails LOUD the first time it is reached; the stale comment tells the next reader NOT TO LOOK AGAIN — it converts an open question into a settled one, silently, and it does so in the file most likely to be trusted.** ⚠⚠ **AND THE DETECTION RECORD IS THE ALARMING PART: BOTH were found ONLY because an unrelated cycle happened to read them.** (a) surfaced when TF-C84's anchor pass chased a `pe64` failure into the pragma table; (b) surfaced when TF-C85 went looking for where `_byteswap_*` was declared. **Nothing in this repo would have found either one on purpose. There is no instrument that reads config prose, so the discovery rate for this defect class is currently a function of luck** — [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] is the sibling: that row is about a guard whose SCOPE is unstated, this row is about a claim with NO guard at all. ★ FIFTH INSTANCE (TF-C88), whose SUB-SHAPE is worse than staleness: `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/sys/ioctl.json`'s `$comment` asserts a no-over-ship rationale, and that FALSE SENTENCE WAS THE JUSTIFICATION FOR THE OMISSION — so it TERMINATED the audit that would have found the missing `_IOC` family, and it omits `_IOWR`, the very macro that fires. A stale FACT rots quietly; a stale JUSTIFICATION actively defends the defect. ⭐ **SIXTH INSTANCE (f), ✔MEASURED 2026-08-12 (inline-asm P1+P2) — AND ITS SUB-SHAPE IS THE ONE THIS PROJECT MEETS MOST OFTEN: A TRUE MEASUREMENT WITH A FALSE INFERENCE FROZEN INTO CONFIG.** ✔TREE `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json`, key `$gnuDunderSpellingsAreUnconditionalComment` (⚠ re-grep the KEY, never the line — it sat at `:492` when the defect was found and at `:518` after the repair, which is [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]] arriving inside this row's own evidence). It OPENED by asserting that *“the `keywords` ROW loop has NO closed key vocabulary … so a `$comment` placed INSIDE a keyword row would load silently and un-typo-guarded”*, and used that as the reason the note lives at top level. ⛔ **That sentence became FALSE the day `kKeywordKeys` landed:** `grammar_schema_json.cpp:3365-3370` declares `kKeywordKeys{"word", "kind", "contextual"}`, runs `DSS_CHECK_KEY_VOCABULARY` over it, and walks EVERY key of EVERY keyword row emitting `C_ConflictingField` for anything outside the set, with `$`-prefixed keys exempted — i.e. the exact guard the comment said did not exist (row [[D-CONFIG-CLOSED-KEY-VOCABULARY-GAPS]]). CORRECTED IN PLACE this cycle: the comment now states the measurement, names what overtook it, and keeps the note at top level for the reason that was always the real one (it argues about TEN rows across two alias groups, so it belongs to no single row). ★★ **NAME THE SHAPE, BECAUSE IT IS NOT ORDINARY STALENESS:** the sentence was TRUE when written and was invalidated by a change in ANOTHER FILE, so no one editing either file was ever looking at both — **nobody re-reads a comment when they change the code it describes**. That is the same shape as UCRT-P5's *“ucrtbase exports no `_setjmp`”*, a true measurement whose false INFERENCE stayed frozen in config for two phases, and it is why instances keep arriving faster than this row's closing work. ⭐ **SEVENTH INSTANCE (g), ✔MEASURED 2026-08-12 (inline-asm P1+P2) — A COMMENT THAT *COUNTS SOMETHING THE CODE DOES NOT CONTAIN*, WHICH IS A SUB-SHAPE NEITHER (a)–(f) COVERS.** ✔TREE `tests/analysis/semantic/test_semantic_analyzer_c_subset.cpp`, the comment above `TEST(SemanticAnalyzerCSubset, InlineAsmLabelSectionWithoutGotoIsRefused)` (⚠ re-grep the TEST NAME, never the line). The retracted sentence, verbatim: *“the three spellings reach the fourth section by three different token routes (all-fused, plain+fused, fused+fused), so each exercises a different tail rule”*. ⛔ ✔MEASURED FALSE off the CST: `("" ::::)` and `("" :: ::)` arrive as the **SAME token pair** — whitespace BETWEEN two fused pairs is lexically irrelevant, so those two share ONE route — and the spelling that would actually have made it three, the ALL-PLAIN `("" : : : : )` that alone reaches `asmLabelsTail` unfused, **was absent from the loop altogether**. Two routes claimed as three, with the third one missing. CORRECTED IN PLACE: the missing form was ADDED (four spellings, three genuine routes), `("" :: ::)` is now labelled the WHITESPACE CONTROL that proves the fusion is lexical rather than written-form, and — the durable half — **each form's route is now CHECKED AGAINST THE CST rather than described in prose**, so a tokenizer or grammar change that re-routes a form goes RED instead of quietly making a sentence false. ★★ **NAME THE SHAPE, IT IS THE REUSABLE PART: A COMMENT THAT COUNTS SOMETHING THE CODE DOES NOT CONTAIN.** (a)–(f) are claims about OTHER files, other corpora, other toolchains — expensive to re-check, which is how they survived. This one was a claim about **the twenty lines directly beneath it**, checkable by counting, and it still shipped: a number in prose reads as a summary rather than as an assertion, so nobody counted. ★ The repair is the general answer to this whole class and the first one in (a)–(g) that is MECHANICAL rather than remembered — **do not assert the count, assert the thing being counted, in the test itself.** ⭐ **SUB-INSTANCE (g2), FLAGGED NOT FIXED — A COMMENT BLOCK THAT CONTRADICTS ITSELF TOP-TO-BOTTOM.** ✔TREE `src/hir/lowering/cst_to_hir.cpp`: the `InlineAsm` arm's comment was corrected at `:5661-5674` to *“UNREACHABLE FROM THE DRIVER, REACHABLE FROM THE TEST TIER — AND HERE IS THE TEST”*, but the SAME block still OPENS twelve lines earlier at `:5649` with the pre-correction heading *“★ WHY THE ASSERTION EXISTS EVEN THOUGH IT CANNOT FIRE TODAY”*. It is an unrelated correctness-of-metadata change that happens to touch the same `u32`.** ⭐ **AND A SECOND HAZARD, ON THE OTHER SIDE: do not let "zero measured effect" argue AGAINST shipping it.
