| `D-SQLITE-PE64-MMAP-PERMUTATION-WINDOWS-CONFOUNDS` | P5 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **P5 ENV / normal — BANDED 2026-08-27 by the P42 registry audit (lane sqlite01), which measured this row; it predates the severity convention and carried no label.** **ATTRIBUTED 2026-07-26 — all 6 failures + 1 abort in the `mmap` permutation are UPSTREAM/PLATFORM, NOT DSS.** They appeared for the FIRST TIME EVER when the corpus resume engine got the pe64 `all` tier past a long-standing abort, so the `mmap` permutation finally executed. EVERY one reproduces BYTE-IDENTICALLY on a native MinGW-GCC build and PASSES on Linux. ★ BOUNDED REPRO (seconds, not hours): `<fixture> <stage>\test\permutations.test mmap backup4.test vacuum2.test wal5.test` from a fresh cwd — DSS 7.4 s / GCC 3.1 s, 6/6 on both; `permutations.test mmap pendingrace.test` aborts in 0.1 s on both. ★★ THE CONTROL IS UNUSUALLY TIGHT, which is why this is an attribution and not a guess: **both executables import the SAME `tcl86.dll`** (`objdump -p`), so `file size` / `open` — the oracle the assertions read — is literally identical code. The two usual confounds are VOID BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by argument: stack reserve 2 MB vs 1 MB (neither overflowed; both reached a normal summary), and CRT — DSS imports msvcrt+ucrtbase mid-migration vs MinGW pure-UCRT, i.e. **two DIFFERENT CRTs produced byte-identical logs**, and the deciding calls are Win32 (`SetEndOfFile` / `CreateFile` / `MapViewOfFile`) issued directly from `os_win.c`. The known oracle weakness [[D-SQLITE-PE64-DSS-CLEANER-THAN-GCC-REFERENCE]] is moot here too: the reference does not merely pass where DSS fails, it FAILS IDENTICALLY, so no cleanliness/coverage asymmetry can be doing the work. Third leg: the Linux GCC build runs the same 460 tests with **0 errors**. ★★ CAUSE 1 — `mm-backup4-3.3` + `mm-vacuum2-3.13`, ONE root cause DEMONSTRATED by a 2x2x2 factorial (`mmap_size` x a second connection holding a mapping): both assertions flip in EXACTLY the same cell (mmap ON **and** a 2nd handle), on BOTH compilers, and in NO cell on Linux. Mechanism: `os_win.c:2124-2125` `winTruncate` calls `SetEndOfFile`, which fails `ERROR_USER_MAPPED_FILE` while any handle holds a section on the file — and the code **DELIBERATELY SWALLOWS that error and returns SQLITE_OK WITHOUT TRUNCATING**. `os_unix.c:3961 unixTruncate` has no such path because POSIX permits `ftruncate` on a mapped file. Both tests assert a file SHRANK. ★ the factorial also REFUTED `nFetchOut` as the trigger (the mmap-on / no-2nd-handle column passes). ★★ CAUSE 2 — `mm-wal5-{pragma,capi}-2.3.{1,2}.8`, ONE root cause seen as FOUR executions of ONE source line (`wal5.test:249`, under the `foreach {testprefix do_wal_checkpoint}` pragma/capi loop x `do_multiclient_test` tn 1,2). Lines 241-248 HARDCODE the unix result with NO OS guard, commented "in mmap() mode, the db is pre-allocated to 2 pages at the start of the checkpoint". MEASURED via `file_control_sizehint_test`, mmap on, hint 2048->4096: Linux **2048->4096 (grows)**; Windows DSS **2048->2048**; Windows GCC **2048->2048**; Windows WITH `file_control_chunksize_test` set **2048->4096** — which isolates the guard exactly. `os_win.c:2670-2687` honours `SQLITE_FCNTL_SIZE_HINT` only `if(pFile->szChunk>0)`, and `szChunk` is set nowhere but `SQLITE_FCNTL_CHUNK_SIZE`, so it is a NO-OP for a main db; `os_unix.c:4094-4106` has an explicit mmap branch that ftruncates up and remaps. Checkpoint never pre-grows the db on Windows -> 1, not 2. ★★ CAUSE 3 — the `pendingrace` ABORT. Control: the raw test with NO permutation (hence no `pragma mmap_size` presql) is rc=0 CLEAN on both toolchains; with mmap it aborts on both and passes on Linux. `pendingrace.test:62-73` `my_db_restore` does a raw Tcl `open test.db w` while connection `db` is still open; on Windows SQLite's live section makes the truncating `CreateFile` fail (`ERROR_USER_MAPPED_FILE`, surfaced by Tcl as EINVAL). The helper is simply not mmap-safe on Windows. ★ the first mechanism guess was REFUTED by probe (a 2-page db reproduced nothing); the 20-page version reproduces the corpus's exact string. ★ WHERE `mm-backup4-3.3` WAS (an open question until now): no exotic marker — a normal two-line `!` pair at `corpus.resume2.log:388718`, i.e. in the ABORTED segment. The engine aborted resume2 at `pendingrace.test` and resumed resume3 at `symlink2.test`; `backup4` sorts before `pendingrace`, `vacuum2`/`wal5` after. A sweep confirms the total is exactly 6 with no seventh hidden anywhere. ★ THE GENERALISATION: these are properties of running SQLite's `mmap` permutation ON WINDOWS AT ALL — any MSVC/MinGW build of 3.54.0 shows them — which strongly suggests upstream does not run `all.test`'s mmap permutation on Windows. ═══ Attributed. Re-verify on any sqlite version bump (upstream may add the missing OS guards). | Add to the harness confound patterns WITH this evidence cited — this is the one case where confound classification is legitimate, because every row has a matched control that flips it on and off. ★ The `pendingrace` ABORT is NOT fully solved by classification, but the residual is SMALLER than first written here (corrected 2026-07-26 by reading the engine, not by recalling it): it costs the remainder of the **aborted FILE only**, NOT the remainder of its segment. VERIFIED in `build-and-test.ps1`: :987-988 records exactly `the REMAINDER of <abortFile> under permutation <perm>` into NOT REACHED — so the loss is named, never silent — while the engine still runs everything after it, via `SQLITE_TEST_PATTERN_LIST` for the rest of that permutation (`Get-FilesAfter`, :714-718, ordinally-after) and `--start=<next>:` for every later permutation (:1022). ★ A second reading trap, checked and CLEARED: :991's "will RE-ATTEMPT it" appears to contradict `Get-FilesAfter`'s exclusive `-gt 0`, but it belongs to the *unresolved-file* branch, where the boundary is the last COMPLETED file — so the unnamed aborting file IS re-attempted there. Both messages are accurate; the two branches carry different boundary semantics. Closing the true residual needs either an upstream fix to `my_db_restore` or an INTRA-FILE restart granularity, which sqlite's `--start=[<perm>:]<file>` does not offer at all. | [[D-SQLITE-PE64-ALL-TIER-OOM-FILE-HANDLE-LEAK]] · [[D-SQLITE-PE64-DSS-CLEANER-THAN-GCC-REFERENCE]] · [[feedback-original-100pct-sqlite-suite]] · sqlite `os_win.c:2124,2670` · `os_unix.c:3961,4094` · `test/wal5.test:241-249` · `test/pendingrace.test:62-73` |
| `D-SQLITE-PE64-ALL-TIER-OOM-FILE-HANDLE-LEAK` | P5 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **P5 ENV / normal — BANDED 2026-08-27 by the P42 registry audit (lane sqlite01), which measured this row; it predates the severity convention and carried no label.** ★★★★★ **POLICY SET 2026-07-26 (user): the ORIGINAL, 100% sqlite suite must be run by our harness — so this is a SURVIVE, never a PATCH.** A staged `tester.tcl` patch was built and PROVEN (bounded repro RC=1 6.0s -> RC=0 3.6s, `0 errors out of 958 tests`, no leaks) and then **fully REVERTED**: patching upstream's test infrastructure means we are no longer running the original suite, and excluding the file means we are not running 100% of it. `DSS_TIER_EXCLUDES` therefore defaults EMPTY. The fix is preserved in the session scratchpad as an UPSTREAM-REPORT deliverable only. Revert verified: `git status` clean, and the WSL clone + the staged tree were confirmed pristine by md5 so the concurrent `all` run was undisturbed. ★ CARRY-FORWARD CONSTRAINTS for the survive-and-resume work (each learned the hard way, all four load-bearing): **(a) NEVER write into the sqlite clone.** `.sh` and `.ps1` share ONE clone (`$HOME/src/sqlite`) but consume it differently — the `.sh` runs the corpus DIRECTLY out of it, the `.ps1` `cp -r`s to a Windows stage. A write there breaks `clone_or_update`'s unguarded `git pull --rebase` on the next `.sh` run AND silently poisons the `.ps1` stage. Resume state belongs in the stage/run dir. **(b) NEVER key resume on a test name or index** — the abort point is NOT stable (`all` tier `.143` vs bounded repro `.134`; the OOM injection point is a function of process-global allocation history). Key on the structural fact 'exited without a summary line'. **(c) Resume MUST be a NEW PROCESS — required for correctness, not convenience.** The leaked handle lives for the fixture process's lifetime; `test.db2` becomes deletable the instant it exits. An in-process continuation would immediately re-hit the same undeletable file. **(d) Do NOT scope the logic to `swarmvtabfault`** — the trigger is the PERMUTATION (`inmemory_journal`'s presql throwing under injected OOM), not the file; any OOM-injecting permutation with a presql can surface the same abort in a different file. ★ Upstream-report nit for whoever files it: the proven fix re-raises with `error $msg`, which discards `errorCode`/`errorInfo`; the canonical Tcl idiom sqlite would more likely accept is `catch {...} msg opts` then `return -options $opts $msg`. Inert across a 34,811-test OOM regression sweep, but worth getting right in the submission. ★★★★ **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-07-26 to the exact line, and the earlier `unionvtab.c` suspect is REFUTED.** CAUSE: sqlite's own `test/tester.tcl:127-129` — the `sqlite3` wrapper proc CREATES the connection (`:127 set res [uplevel 1 sqlite_orig $args]`, at which point `test.db2` is open) and then lets `:129 [lindex $args 0] eval $::G(perm:presql)` THROW STRAIGHT THROUGH, never closing what it just created. `swarmvtabfault.test:26`'s `sqlite3 dbX $file` therefore throws, `:30 dbX close` is skipped, the handle survives, and the NEXT iteration's `db_restore` (`tester.tcl:2471`, `foreach f [glob test.db*] { forcedelete $f }`) hits ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED. ★ THE TRIGGER IS THE PERMUTATION'S PRESQL, NOT swarmvtab: `inmemory_journal` sets `::G(perm:presql)` to `pragma journal_mode='memory'`. Standalone `swarmvtabfault.test` and `permutations.test full swarmvtab%` never set it — which is exactly why both earlier probes passed and this looked irreproducible. ★★ BOUNDED REPRO — **6 SECONDS**, deterministic, on BOTH fixtures: `testfixture.exe <testdir>/permutations.test inmemory_journal swarmvtabfault%` (aborts at `.134`; the `all`-tier run said `.143` — same mechanism, the index is a function of process-global allocation history). This replaced a 2.5-HOUR reproduction. ★ DECISIVE EVIDENCE (crime-scene probe): at the instant `forcedelete test.db2` fails — `dbX` command exists = 1; `dbX close` -> **rc=0**; immediately retried `forcedelete test.db2` -> **rc=0**, file gone. Plus: exactly ONE `presql THREW 'out of memory'` in the whole run, immediately before the failure; and of 103 open-throws across the sweep, 102 took the clean `tclsqlite.c:4411-4425` path (empty message, `dbX` never created) while exactly one — iteration 133 — had message `out of memory` AND `dbX` existing, which can only come from the WRAPPER since `DbMain` registers the command as its final action. ★ WHY `unionvtab.c` IS REFUTED (4 independent grounds, keep these — the hypothesis was plausible and is worth being able to re-kill): (1) closing `dbX` ALONE releases the file; a second orphaned handle would have kept the retry failing. (2) `dbX close` returned rc=0, NOT SQLITE_BUSY — the anchor's own premise (`sqlite3_close` leaves the handle open on unfinalized statements) never held here. (3) `test.db2` is 0 BYTES at failure: `fetch_db` died before its `CREATE TABLE`, so `unionOpenDatabaseInner` never reached `sqlite3_open_v2` on it that iteration. (4) With `unionvtab.c` UNTOUCHED and only `tester.tcl` fixed, the 479+479 sweep ends `All memory allocations freed - no leaks` — an orphaned connection (pointer zeroed, unreachable) WOULD have surfaced as leaked allocations. The three ignored `sqlite3_close()` returns at unionvtab.c:470/488/688 (+626) are REAL but are not what fires here. ★ MINIMAL FIX, PROVEN on both fixtures: wrap the three post-open config steps (`presql`, `dbconfig`, `cache size 3`) in a `catch`; on error `catch { <handle> close }` then rethrow. DSS RC=1 -> RC=0 958/958 no leaks; native gcc RC=1 -> RC=0 958/958 no leaks; regression `inmemory_journal x malloc%\|mallocA%\|oom%` = 21 files, **34,811 tests, 0 errors**, no leaks, 97s. Orthogonally confirmed: fixing ONLY `fetch_db` also goes green — either site closes the hole, proving `dbX` is the sole holder. Prefer the `tester.tcl` fix: the wrapper creates the resource and abandons it; `fetch_db` is the victim. Lines 133 and 135 carry the same hazard. ★ UPSTREAM-REPORTABLE: SQLite 3.54.0, fossil `2f0bde4bc89c2b088c8f821719d7e457d9129c0493032152b8f4472673973e58` (git mirror `701092f4a5`); `tester.tcl`/`swarmvtabfault.test`/`permutations.test`/`malloc_common.tcl` are byte-identical (LF-normalized md5) to that checkout, so this is upstream's bug and not a DSS staging artifact. It is a TEST-HARNESS bug, not a library bug, and Windows-only in effect (Linux `unlink()` on an open file succeeds). ★ CORRECTIONS to this row's earlier text: 'aborts at the -prep step' is true as reported but MISLEADING — the leak happens in the PREVIOUS iteration's `-body`; `-prep` runs with injection OFF (`malloc_common.tcl:363` precedes `:369 eval $O(-injectstart)`), so prep cannot be the leaking step. And the failing `forcedelete` is `db_restore`'s (`tester.tcl:2471`), not `swarmvtabfault.test:25`'s. ★★★ **ATTRIBUTED 2026-07-26 — NOT A DSS DEFECT. The native gcc testfixture fails IDENTICALLY.** Matched control run through the SAME `all` tier: native `... Ok` 3,583,595 vs DSS 3,584,676 / 3,584,674; last test passed `inmemory_journal.swarmvtabfault-1.1-oom-persistent.142` in ALL THREE; same error, and a **byte-identical traceback** differing only in the executable name on line 1. ★ SHARPER THAN A BARE TIE: the fault SCHEDULES coincide exactly — max OOM iteration reached per permutation, native vs DSS, `full` 492/492, `memsubsys1` 541/541, `memsubsys2` 549/549. Neither binary was spared or doomed by a shifted allocation sequence; both inject at the same allocation and leak at the same one. ★ The CRT-mismatch caveat (DSS msvcrt+ucrtbase vs MinGW pure-UCRT) is MOOT here, and the reasoning is worth keeping: it was the first alternative explanation ONLY IF native had SURVIVED. Two runs that AGREE cannot be explained by a variable that DIFFERS between them. ★ MECHANISM, corroborated three ways: (1) the leftover 0-byte `test.db2` deletes cleanly once the process EXITS ⇒ the holder was IN-PROCESS, not an external scanner; (2) it reproduces in a pure gcc build ⇒ sqlite's own code; (3) `test.db` and `test.db1` delete fine and only `test.db2` fails ⇒ the glob-order signature of ONE leaked handle. PRIME SUSPECT `ext/misc/unionvtab.c` — the return of `sqlite3_close()` is IGNORED at lines 470 (`unionCloseSources`), 488 (`unionDisconnect`) and 688 (`unionOpenDatabase` error path), each followed by `pSrc->db = 0`. `sqlite3_close()` returns SQLITE_BUSY and leaves the handle OPEN when statements are unfinalized — precisely the state an OOM-aborted swarmvtab cursor teardown leaves — and zeroing the pointer then makes that connection unreachable for the process lifetime. Linux `unlink()` succeeds on an open file, which is why upstream CI never sees it. ⇒ RESOLUTION: an upstream sqlite-on-Windows defect. It may be classified as a confound — but ONLY with this control as the evidence, and NOT by declaring an ABORTED run green: the abort kills the remaining corpus, so calling it green would claim coverage over files that never ran (the same overstatement caught earlier on the `full` tier). The correct fix is to let the REST of `all` run. ★ NOTE the brief's premise that no bounded reproduction exists is probably WRONG: the failure occurs in the **`inmemory_journal`** permutation, which neither earlier probe exercised (they ran the test standalone and under `permutations.test full swarmvtab%`). `testfixture permutations.test inmemory_journal swarmvtab%` is the untried, minutes-long candidate. **OPEN (created 2026-07-25). The pe64 `all` tier does NOT complete — but the failure is neither a wrong answer nor a crash.** **3,584,676 tests pass with ZERO `Expected:`/`Got:` mismatches**, then the fixture dies on a TCL error, not a signal: `testfixture.exe: error deleting "test.db2": permission denied`, raised from `forcedelete` ← `db_restore` ← `faultsim_restore_and_reopen`, inside `swarmvtabfault-1.1-oom-persistent` (test/swarmvtabfault.test:47 — OOM fault injection at allocation #1,000,000 against the `unionvtab` extension). The immediately preceding ~142 cases of that same test all pass. ★★ **REPRODUCIBILITY CONFIRMED 2026-07-26 — 2 runs of 2, same failure, same place.** Run 1: 3,584,676 `Ok` lines; run 2: 3,584,674 — a two-line delta across 3.58M tests (randomised cases), identical error text, identical test. ⇒ This is a DETERMINISTIC bug, NOT a flake and NOT antivirus. That matters procedurally as much as technically: the FIRST native-toolchain control was run before reproducibility was established and came back VOID — standalone `swarmvtabfault.test` passes on BOTH binaries (956 tests, reaching `.477`, i.e. 3.4x past the `.142` where the full run dies), and all three memory permutations (`full`/`memsubsys1`/`memsubsys2`) diff CLEAN between DSS and gcc with byte-identical allocator totals. A void control is not an acquittal; it only proved no bounded window reproduces it. ★ WHAT THAT VOID CONTROL DID ESTABLISH (a real constraint): iterations 1-141 of the failing test each ran `db_restore` -> `forcedelete test.db*` SUCCESSFULLY, so the leaked handle is acquired DURING `swarmvtabfault`'s own execution, not inherited from an earlier file — the trigger needs allocator/VFS state accumulated across the ~813 preceding files (many register and unregister test VFSes), which is exactly why no bounded window reproduces it. ★ ALSO FLAGGED BY THAT CONTROL — a real flaw in the comparison, not a nitpick: the native binary is NOT CRT-matched. DSS imports `msvcrt.dll` + `ucrtbase.dll` (mid-migration, [[D-FFI-PE-CRT-UCRT-MIGRATION]]); MinGW imports the pure-UCRT `api-ms-win-crt-*` set. If the leak is CRT-mediated, a native binary could survive for a reason unrelated to codegen — the exact trap this arc already fell into once. sqlite's `os_win.c` uses raw Win32 `CreateFileW`/`CloseHandle` for DB files (so the CRT is not in the path for the `test.db2` handle itself), but the Tcl layer and `fopen`-based code such as `ext/misc/fileio.c` DO use it. ★ THE POSIX/WINDOWS SEMANTIC AT ISSUE: Linux `unlink()` succeeds on an OPEN file (the inode survives until the last fd closes), Windows refuses to delete a file that any handle still holds. So a handle that leaks under OOM is INVISIBLE on the elf legs and fatal here — which is exactly why `full`/`all` are green on Linux and this is not. ★★ WHY THIS IS PROBABLY NOT ENVIRONMENTAL, i.e. do NOT dismiss it as antivirus: sqlite's own `tester.tcl` already knows this spot is Windows-fragile — `do_delete_file` retries, and `getFileRetries` returns **50 on Windows** (0 elsewhere) with `getFileRetryDelay` = **100 ms**. That is **50 retries over 5 FULL SECONDS**, and the delete STILL failed. AV "tag-alongs" (the cause sqlite's own comment cites) do not hold a handle for 5 s. The parsimonious reading is a genuinely LEAKED file handle under injected OOM. ★ WHAT IS NOT YET KNOWN — run the control BEFORE attributing it: does a NATIVE Windows testfixture survive the same test? The pe64 stack-depth investigation taught this the hard way ([[D-SQLITE-PE64-FULL-TIER-STACK-DEPTH]] METHOD WARNING): comparing DSS against an unmatched native build produced the WRONG conclusion, and the native binary must be built and driven identically. Candidate causes to separate: (a) DSS/consumer leaks a handle on an OOM unwind path; (b) sqlite-on-Windows genuinely leaks under injected OOM and upstream never sees it because its CI is Linux; (c) something in the mid-flight CRT split ([[D-FFI-PE-CRT-UCRT-MIGRATION]]) — NOTE sqlite's os_win.c uses Win32 `CreateFile`/`CloseHandle` directly, NOT CRT `fopen`, so (c) is a priori unlikely and should be TESTED not assumed; the TCL layer, however, does use CRT/Tcl channel I/O. ═══ Already fired (the pe64 `all` tier is red today). Note `full` (0/979,808) and `veryquick` (0/330,937) are BOTH green — this is `all`-tier-only, so it gates only the deepest claim. | (1) Run the native-testfixture control on `swarmvtabfault.test` alone — cheap, one file, and it decides attribution. (2) Per the outcome: chase the leaking handle on the OOM unwind path, or classify as an upstream-on-Windows confound WITH the control as evidence (never on assertion — "green by relabelling" is forbidden). (3) If it is ours, a fail-loud pin on the close path is the durable fix, not a retry bump. | [[D-SQLITE-PE64-FULL-TIER-STACK-DEPTH]] · [[D-FFI-PE-CRT-UCRT-MIGRATION]] · sqlite `test/swarmvtabfault.test:47` · `test/tester.tcl` (`do_delete_file`/`getFileRetries`) · commit `dd3adc65` (full tier green) |

| `D-LK-MACHO-STACK-RESERVE-LC-MAIN` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **P2 DIVERGENT / LOW — BANDED 2026-08-27 by the P42 registry audit (lane link02), which measured this row; it predates the severity convention and carried no label.** **OPEN (created 2026-07-25). A NAMED COMPILER GAP, not a platform limit — the distinction is the whole point of this row.** Mach-O exec CAN express a per-program stack reserve: `LC_MAIN` carries a `stacksize` field (uint64) exactly as PE's optional header carries `SizeOfStackReserve`. DSS's Mach-O walker does not write it, so both `macho64-arm64-darwin-exec.format.json` and `macho64-x86_64-darwin-exec.format.json` declare `"stackReserveUnsupportedReason": "walker-not-implemented"` and a stack-reserve request on a Darwin target is REFUSED LOUDLY rather than silently dropped. ★ WHY IT IS ITS OWN VERB (and its own anchor): the other three refusal reasons are facts about the platform that will never change — `runtime-controlled` (ELF: the kernel sizes the stack from `ulimit -s`; nothing the linker writes matters), `loader-ignores-field` (PE DLL: the field exists but Windows reads the host EXE's instead), `no-image-field` (staticlib / relocatable object: no header to carry it). This one is OURS and is closable. Lumping it in with the genuine platform limits would let a DSS shortfall wear a "that is just how macOS works" disguise and never get fixed — the `walker-not-implemented` verb exists specifically to prevent that, and this row is what makes it tracked rather than merely well-worded. ★ CONTEXT on urgency: Darwin gives the main thread ~8 MB (secondary pthreads ~512 KB by default) — DOCUMENTED, not measured here, no Mac on this box — so macOS is far from the 1 MB Windows cliff that forced [[D-SQLITE-PE64-FULL-TIER-STACK-DEPTH]]. Combined with [[D-CODEGEN-FRAME-SIZE-VS-NATIVE-2X]] (DSS frames ~2x native) the headroom is still ~4x better than pe64's was, so this is LOW priority until a real consumer appears. ═══ Whichever comes first: a macOS program needs deep recursion (a `--project` build requesting `stackReserve` on a Darwin target — today that is a hard refusal, and the diagnostic already names this gap), OR a secondary-thread workload hits the 512 KB pthread default, OR the Mach-O walker is being touched for another reason and the arm is cheap to add alongside. | Implement the walker arm: write the requested reserve into `LC_MAIN.stacksize` in the Mach-O writer; flip both Darwin exec `.format.json`s from `stackReserveUnsupportedReason` to a real declared capability (the PE `stackReserveVehicle` pattern); pin it the same way PE is pinned — assert the NON-DEFAULT value reaches the emitted `LC_MAIN` bytes through the real pipeline (the "knob that lies" guard), plus red-on-disable. RUNTIME closure needs a Mac (no off-Mac emulator for Mach-O — the standing cross-target rule), so gate the CLOSED mark on the `macos-latest` CI leg, never on local-green. | [[D-SQLITE-PE64-FULL-TIER-STACK-DEPTH]] · [[D-CODEGEN-FRAME-SIZE-VS-NATIVE-2X]] · `src/core/types/object_format_kind.hpp` (`StackReserveUnsupportedReason::WalkerNotImplemented`) · `src/dss-config/object-formats/macho64-{arm64,x86_64}-darwin-exec.format.json` · `src/link/format/macho.cpp` |

| `D-LANG-UAC-UNSIGNED-COUNTERPART-OF-SIGNED` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN (⚠ **THE GATE FIRED AND NOBODY RE-READ THE ROW** — the opener this row names has since CLOSED. Re-verdicted 2026-09-03, cycle P56, by the burn-down of [[D-GATE-ANCHOR-BALANCE-ARM-6-CANNOT-SEE-AN-OPEN-VS-GATED-CONTRADICTION]]; the STATUS cell already read OPEN, only this word did not.) (surfaced by [[D-LANG-TYPE-IDENTITY-VOCABULARY]], 2026-07-20; NOT a miscompile — a NAME-only divergence at identical representation).** C 6.3.1.8's FOURTH mixed-signedness branch ("otherwise, both operands are converted to the unsigned integer type CORRESPONDING TO the type of the operand with signed integer type") is unreachable in DSS: `usualArithmeticCommonType` picks the winning KIND from the width-rank lattice, so `aLong + anUnsignedInt` under LLP64 (I32 vs U32, but rank(long) > rank(unsigned int)) yields `unsigned int` where C says `unsigned long`. Both are U32 — the VALUE, width, and codegen are identical; only the resulting type's NAME differs, observable solely through `_Generic` on such a sum. The type-identity fix scoped it out deliberately: its tie-break fires only when the two operands share a KIND, and "when kinds differ, existing behavior is unchanged" was the stated boundary (pre-fix, `long`≡`int` and `unsigned long`≡`unsigned int` made the two answers the SAME TypeId, so the gap was invisible). NOT on the SQLite path. **Trigger:** a consumer whose `_Generic` (or `typeof`) dispatches on a mixed-signedness sum of a named and an anonymous integer entry at the same width. **Closing work:** declare each signed vocabulary entry's UNSIGNED COUNTERPART in the language config (a `unsignedCounterpart` key on the `typeSpecifiers` row, resolved at load like every other name), then implement branch 4 in `usualArithmeticCommonType`'s `RankPreferUnsigned` arm: when the unsigned operand's declared rank is LOWER than the signed operand's and the signed type cannot represent the unsigned range, return the signed entry's counterpart. Priority: **low**. Pin (BOTH data models — the gap is not LLP64-specific, and citing only one example understated it): (i) LLP64 — `_Generic((aLong + anUnsignedInt), unsigned long: 1, default: 0)` → 1; (ii) ★ LP64 — `_Generic((aLongLong + anUnsignedLong), unsigned long long: 1)` currently FAILS TO COMPILE (`S002A` controlling type matches no association, CLI-confirmed on `x86_64:elf64-x86_64-linux-exec`): `long long` and `unsigned long` are both 64-bit on LP64, so the width lattice picks the unsigned operand's own entry and yields `unsigned long`, where C 6.3.1.8 branch 4 requires the UNSIGNED COUNTERPART of the higher-ranked SIGNED type, i.e. `unsigned long long` (a 64-bit `long long` cannot represent every `unsigned long`). Same VALUE and width either way — only the resulting type's NAME differs. | (open) | [[D-LANG-TYPE-IDENTITY-VOCABULARY]] (the fix that made this observable); `src/analysis/semantic/type_rules.hpp` (`usualArithmeticCommonType`); plan 23 full-C arc |
| `D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-VOCABULARY-TAG-RESIDUALS` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN (⚠ **THE GATE FIRED AND NOBODY RE-READ THE ROW** — the opener this row names has since CLOSED. Re-verdicted 2026-09-03, cycle P56, by the burn-down of [[D-GATE-ANCHOR-BALANCE-ARM-6-CANNOT-SEE-AN-OPEN-VS-GATED-CONTRADICTION]]; the STATUS cell already read OPEN, only this word did not.) (opened by the [[D-LANG-TYPE-IDENTITY-VOCABULARY]] follow-up sweep, 2026-07-20).** The sweep tagged every shipped descriptor entry whose C type is UNAMBIGUOUSLY a named vocabulary entry on the formats that descriptor ships on. These were DELIBERATELY LEFT ANONYMOUS, each for a stated reason, and each is a *name-only* gap (the width/ABI is unaffected; it bites only on a POINTER to the entry or a `_Generic` over it): (a) `sys/types.json`'s `dev_t`/`ino_t`/`nlink_t`/`blksize_t`/`blkcnt_t`/`suseconds_t` — glibc spells them `unsigned long`/`long`, but the shipped u64/i64 already mis-models the Darwin widths (`dev_t` is `__int32_t`, `nlink_t` is `__uint16_t`, …), so a name-only tag would add a SECOND lie on top of a width lie; closing them means fixing the macho representations first. (b) `time.json`'s `struct timespec` — `tv_sec` is `long` on glibc/Darwin but `__int64` (`long long`) on the MSVC CRT, and `tv_nsec` is a 32-bit `long` there while the descriptor models both as i64; the struct is FLAT (all formats), so tagging needs the same per-format width fix. (c) `pthread.json`'s `pthread_t` and `threads.json`'s `thrd_t` — glibc `unsigned long`, Darwin an opaque POINTER modelled as u64. (d) `stdlib.json`'s `atol`/`strtol` (result), `strtoul` (result) and `labs` (arg + result) — the C `long`/`unsigned long` type, declared per-data-model via `signatureByDataModel` so the WIDTH is right on both models, but both arms are still ANONYMOUS (`i64`/`i32`, `u64`/`u32`); a caller doing `long *p = &r; ... strtol(...)` into a `long` is unaffected (by-value converts), but `_Generic((strtol(s,e,10)), long: ...)` takes no arm. (e) `sys/resource.json`'s 14 `ru_*` counters (`ru_maxrss` … `ru_nivcsw`) — glibc/Darwin spell every one `long int`, all still bare `i64`; the embedded `timeval` fields WERE tagged (c-round-3, the include-order fix), so this descriptor is now internally mixed — harmless (the counters are read by value) but worth closing together. (f) `time.json`'s `struct timespec` (`tv_sec`/`tv_nsec`, both bare `i64`) — same blocker as the `<time.h>` row above: the struct is FLAT across all three formats while the MSVC CRT spells `tv_sec` as `__int64` (`long long`) and `tv_nsec` as a 32-bit `long`, so tagging needs the per-format width fix FIRST. (g) `sys/types.json`'s `off_t` **pe** arm — the elf (`i64 "long"`) and macho (`i64 "long long"`) arms are tagged but the pe arm is a bare `i64`; MSVC's `_off_t` is a 32-bit `long` and `_off64_t` is `__int64` (`long long`), so the pe arm needs a WIDTH decision (which of the two `<sys/types.h>` spellings DSS models) before it can be tagged — unlike its `ssize_t` sibling, whose LLP64 answer is unambiguously `long long`. (h) windows.json's BY-VALUE `DWORD`/`LONG` scalar parameters and returns — left anonymous because a by-value position converts implicitly in BOTH directions (so the tag is cosmetic there) while a mis-tag from a guessed SDK type would be a real false error. ★ RE-MEASURED 2026-08-03 (the SQLite-CLI descriptor cycle): this class's original scope claim — *"the POINTER and STRUCT-FIELD positions, which are strict TypeId identity, were all tagged"* — is **REFUTED and REPLACED**, and the "~60 signatures" estimate was never measured. Actual counts (measured, `$comment` line excluded): 46 rows carry at least one tagged `u32 "unsigned long"`, 126 rows carry at least one bare `u32` — but the bare rows are NOT one population. By section: `structs` **0 bare / 29 tagged** (the struct-field half of the claim HOLDS — every field is tagged); `typedefs` 1 bare / 12 tagged (the 1 is `UINT`, which is **correct** — Win32 `UINT` is `unsigned int`, deliberately anonymous because c.lang.json names only the `long`/`long long`/`__int128`/`long double` families); `constants` 81 bare / 0 (a constant's `type` slot, a different position class); `symbols` **44 bare / 5 tagged** — those 44 are the real residual. ★ THE GAP THE OLD CLAIM MISSED is a THIRD identity position its taxonomy had no bucket for: a **NESTED FUNCTION-POINTER PARAMETER**. windows.json:324 spelled `SetConsoleCtrlHandler`'s callback `ptr<fn(u32) -> i32>` with a bare `u32` where the SDK's `PHANDLER_ROUTINE` takes `DWORD`. A function designator decaying to `Ptr<FnSig>` is matched by INTERNER IDENTITY (`src/analysis/semantic/type_rules.hpp:520-525` — `lhsElem[0] == rhs`, no structural walk), and a FnSig is interned from its operand TypeIds (`src/core/types/type_lattice/type_lattice.cpp:867-900`), so ONE untagged nested param makes the entire signature a different TypeId. MEASURED: sqlite `shell.c`'s `static BOOL WINAPI ConsoleCtrlHandler(DWORD)` (:27115) failed `error[S0003] got ConsoleCtrlHandler` — the last pe64 blocker for the SQLite CLI; minimal repro `BOOL WINAPI H(DWORD)` red vs `int WINAPI H(unsigned int)` clean. FIXED at :324 to `ptr<fn(u32 "unsigned long") -> i32>`; :324 was the ONLY `ptr<fn(` in the file, so that shape is exhausted **today**. The other 43 bare-`u32` symbol rows are deliberately NOT mass-edited — a blanket sweep is unreviewable and could itself break representation-compatible call sites — and they are genuinely mixed: most really are `DWORD` (`Sleep`, `GetLastError`, `GetStdHandle`, `HeapAlloc`'s `dwFlags`, `WaitForSingleObject`'s `dwMilliseconds`), a minority really are `UINT` and are correctly bare (`MultiByteToWideChar`/`WideCharToMultiByte`'s `UINT CodePage`) — which is precisely why each needs its SDK type VERIFIED, never guessed. ★ STRUCT FIELDS — asked and answered definitively, code-grounded (`type_lattice.cpp:641-644` + `:392-448`; `src/analysis/semantic/semantic_analyzer.cpp:9934-9936`; `type_rules.hpp:280-282`): the two senses SPLIT. (i) a field's spelling IS load-bearing for the ENCLOSING STRUCT's TypeId — `contentDeclSiteKey` mixes each field's TypeId, so `struct "X" { u32 }` and `struct "X" { u32 "unsigned long" }` intern DIFFERENTLY, and `ffi::ShippedTypeConsistency` fails loud `F_ShippedTypeIdentityConflict` when two declarations disagree. (ii) but a BY-VALUE read or write of a scalar field is REPRESENTATION-compatible — member access stamps the field's TypeId and the ordinary `isAssignable` unsigned-rank arm then accepts it — so `unsigned int x = si.dwPageSize;` is clean. Only taking the field's ADDRESS is identity-strict. ⇒ the "struct fields are strict identity" shorthand was true only in sense (i), and stating it unqualified is what made the nested-fn-ptr hole invisible. ★ ALSO CORRECTED in the same edit: windows.json's own `$comment` TYPE-MAPPINGS prose read *"DWORD/UINT/ULONG → u32; LONG/BOOL/INT → i32"*, flatly contradicting the typedefs block it purports to describe (:12-14 tag LONG/ULONG/DWORD). That contradiction was load-bearing rather than cosmetic — it is the prose that licensed the bare rows below it — and now states the tagged forms plus why UINT/BOOL/INT correctly stay bare. **Trigger (what fires the NEXT instance, in likelihood order):** for (h) — (1) ANY new windows.json row that grows a nested `ptr<fn(...)>` parameter, or an existing one gaining a callback, where a param is `DWORD`/`LONG` in the SDK: re-check every fn-ptr row added from here, because the "only one such row" property is a fact about today's file, not an invariant; (2) a consumer taking the ADDRESS of one of the 43 bare-`u32` by-value positions and assigning it to `DWORD *`/`LPDWORD`; (3) a bare-`u32` type reused as a NEW struct field (fields enter the enclosing struct's TypeId, and `F_ShippedTypeIdentityConflict` will catch the disagreement loudly); (4) a `_Generic` over one. For (a)-(g) — the same address-of / correspondingly-spelled-pointer / `_Generic` triggers as before. **Closing work:** per group above — (a)/(b)/(f)/(g) need the per-format representation (or width) decision FIRST, then a `when:{format}`/`when:{dataModel}` tag; (c) needs a decision on modelling Darwin's opaque handle; (d)/(e) are mechanical tag sweeps (the widths are already right, only the identity name is missing); (h) is a mechanical sweep once each parameter's SDK type is verified rather than inferred. ★ Every one of these is now MACHINE-GUARDED against becoming INCONSISTENT while it stays open: `ffi::ShippedTypeConsistency` (compile-time, `F_ShippedTypeIdentityConflict`) plus the exhaustive `tests/ffi/test_shipped_type_consistency.cpp` sweep reject any two declarations of one tag/typedef that disagree for a target, and any tag whose width the active data model cannot produce — so a PARTIAL tag sweep can no longer silently split a type in two. Priority: **low** (name-only, no width/ABI/codegen effect). | (open) | opened by [[D-LANG-TYPE-IDENTITY-VOCABULARY]]; `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/` (sys/types.json, time.json, pthread.json, threads.json, windows.json); plan 11 FFI / plan 25 per-target structs |
| `D-CORE-OFFSET-CLAMP-FAMILY-INCOMPLETE` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **SWEPT AND CLEAR 2026-07-28 (TF-C80) — ★ a CLAMPING-FAMILY CLASS row. It exists so the LSP twin is NOT re-filed as a defect, and so the CONTRAST that explains the whole incident is not lost.** The audit item raised against this family was `src/lsp/diagnostic_translator.cpp:38`, which performs the structurally identical `buffer.text().substr(range.startByte, range.endByte - range.startByte)` on the LSP path. ✔TREE MEASURED — **it is NOT vulnerable**: its range comes from the SHARED helper `lineByteRangeFor` (`src/lsp/utf16_column.cpp:50-70`), which **clamps FIRST** (`byteOffset <= text.size() ? byteOffset : text.size()`, :58-60) and only then walks both directions from the clamped position, so `start <= clamped <= end <= text.size()` holds and the length subtraction cannot underflow. The same clearance covers its two sibling call sites, `lsp_semantic_query.cpp:33` and `:44`. **No work is required at any LSP site; do not "harden" them.** ★★ **THE LESSON IS THE CONTRAST, and it is worth more than either site individually.** The LSP path is safe **because it was CONSOLIDATED into one clamped helper** — that helper's own comment (utf16_column.cpp:52-56) records that it replaced "two divergent implementations previously kept in diagnostic_translator.cpp and lsp_semantic_query.cpp" so that end-of-buffer clamping "behaves identically on every path". `extractLine` is the site that kept a PRIVATE COPY of the same walk, and it is the site that broke, with a heap over-read. **The duplicated bounds walk is the defect; the shared clamped helper is the fix shape.** ✔TREE **FAMILY SWEEP over `src/`:** the BACKWARD newline walk — the only shape that can run off the FRONT of a buffer, and the shape that failed — exists in exactly TWO places: `diagnostic_reporter.cpp:346` (was unclamped; fixed by TF-C80) and `utf16_column.cpp:62` (clamped). Every other `'\n'` walk in `src/` is a FORWARD walk already bounded by `< size()` (`lir_text.cpp:575`, `hir_text.cpp:1123`, `mir_text.cpp:779`, `ar_reader.cpp:132`) and is not of this class. The clamped offset-consumer family itself is `SourceBuffer::lineCol` (source_buffer.cpp:115), `SourceBuffer::slice` (:105-111), `SourceReader::slice` (source_reader.cpp:19-25) and now `extractLine`. | Trigger: a THIRD line-window walk being written, or a CR-only source rendering differently in the editor than on the CLI. Closing work — there are two residuals, neither urgent, and NEITHER is the LSP `substr`: (a) **DUPLICATION GUARD** — when a third line-window walk is needed, route it through a shared clamped helper instead of copying the loop a third time; the two survivors are close enough to unify that a reviewer should ask whether the reporter can simply adopt `lineByteRangeFor`. (b) ★ **AN UNRECONCILED DIVERGENCE BETWEEN THE TWO SURVIVING WALKS, flagged by TF-C80 and NOT fixed by it:** on the BACKWARD walk the LSP helper stops at `'\n'` OR `'\r'` (utf16_column.cpp:62) while the reporter stops at `'\n'` only (diagnostic_reporter.cpp:346) — so a bare-CR line break yields a different line window in the editor than on the command line. Both are memory-safe; this is a CORRECTNESS-of-position question, not a safety one. Its real-world reach is UNMEASURED — **measure it** against a CR-only source before deciding it matters, and do not quote a diagnostic count as the justification. | plan-00 §0.1 TF-C80 · CLEARED site `src/lsp/diagnostic_translator.cpp:38` via `src/lsp/utf16_column.cpp:50-70` `lineByteRangeFor` · sibling LSP callers `src/lsp/lsp_semantic_query.cpp:33` + `:44` · the incident this generalises [[D-DIAG-RENDERER-PAST-END-SPAN-HEAP-OVERREAD]] · producer-side defect [[D-PP-REMAP-ORIGIN-OFFSET-UNVALIDATED]] · clamped family `source_buffer.cpp:105-115` + `source_reader.cpp:19-25` |

| `D-PP-IF-OPERAND-PARSE-NO-SHORTCIRCUIT` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★★ A GENERAL C11 §6.10.1 CONFORMANCE DEFECT, and the ROOT that [[D-PP-HAS-EXTENSION-BUILTIN-ABSENT]] is only one symptom of. | `D-CSUBSET-BUILTIN-SHADOWED-BY-PROTOTYPE-REVERTS-TO-EXTERN` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-07-31 (TF-C106), found by the IMPLEMENTING agent while probing, not by a failing gate.** ★ **MEASURED: a builtin declared in `builtinFunctions[]` is SHADOW-ABLE, so a translation unit that merely PROTOTYPES the same name turns every call back into an ORDINARY EXTERN REFERENCE.** The agent reproduced it deliberately: prototyping `_byteswap_ushort` in the TU produced **3× `K_SymbolUndefined`** where the builtin lowering had been emitting `bswap` instructions. ⓘ **Shadowing is the CORRECT C semantics** (a user declaration must win over a compiler builtin — that is why the mechanism exists), so this row is NOT a bug report against the shadowing rule. **THE HAZARD IS THE FAILURE MODE:** the call silently stops being a lowered instruction and becomes a link/load-time dependency on a symbol that, for this family, **DOES NOT EXIST** — `msvcrt.dll` exports no `_byteswap_*` (measured by `objdump -p`, with memcpy/_msize/puts as positive controls), and DSS eager-imports declared symbols, so the pe64 outcome is `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND (0xC0000139)` AT LOAD rather than a compile error. ⚠ **SQLITE IS SAFE TODAY, and only by a hair:** it reaches these names through `#pragma intrinsic(...)`, which NAMES without DECLARING (`realizationRequestOnly`) — so nothing prototypes them. ★★ **THE LIVE TRAP: a REAL MSVC `<intrin.h>` DOES prototype the whole family, and `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/intrin.json` is deliberately a near-empty stub.** Anyone who "completes" that descriptor by adding the honest prototypes — an obviously reasonable-looking improvement — silently converts every byteswap in the corpus from an instruction into an unresolvable import, and the pe64 leg dies at LOAD with no compile diagnostic. That is precisely the shape [[D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-EAGER-IMPORT]] exists to warn about, arriving from the opposite direction. | (1) Put a LOUD warning in `intrin.json`'s `$comment` stating that the `_byteswap_*` / `__builtin_bswap*` family must NEVER be given prototypes there, and WHY (prototype ⇒ shadow ⇒ extern ⇒ eager import ⇒ 0xC0000139 at load, because no CRT exports them). (2) Decide the general policy and make it enforceable: either (a) DIAGNOSE the collision — when a user/descriptor declaration shadows a builtin whose lowering has NO importable symbol behind it, emit a real warning naming both, so the silent conversion becomes visible; or (b) let a descriptor mark such names UNSHADOWABLE. ★ (a) is preferred: it keeps C's shadowing semantics intact and only makes the CONSEQUENCE visible, which is the fail-loud posture this project takes elsewhere. (3) Red-on-disable is cheap and exact: a TU that prototypes `_byteswap_ushort` and calls it must produce the chosen diagnostic; today it produces `K_SymbolUndefined` at best and a LOAD failure at worst. Trigger: FIRED (reproduced on demand); no corpus consumer today. Priority: normal — nothing is broken now, but the trap is baited and the bait looks like an improvement. | [[D-CSUBSET-INTRINSIC-BSWAP]] (the family that made it reachable) · [[D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-EAGER-IMPORT]] (why an undeclared-but-imported symbol is fatal at LOAD, not link) · `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/intrin.json` (the stub that must stay a stub for this family) |

| `D-PP-DATETIME-BUF-SIZED-FOR-VALID-TM-ONLY` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **FIX LANDED 2026-08-03 — VERIFICATION PENDING on the host that surfaced it. ★ THE FIRST FINDING PRODUCED BY THE NATIVE arm64 HOST, AND IT IS A FINDING ABOUT THE HOST AS AN INSTRUMENT AS MUCH AS ABOUT THE CODE.** ✔MEASURED: building DSS with gcc-13 Release on real aarch64 hardware (Ubuntu 24.04, the Oracle VPS commissioned 2026-08-03) emits `-Wformat-truncation` at `src/analysis/preprocess/preprocessor.cpp:4530` — *"`__builtin___snprintf_chk` output 9 or more bytes (assuming 19) into a destination of size 16"* — for the `__DATE__` spelling in `MacroExpander::computeDateTime`. **The x86_64 legs (Windows MSVC/Ninja-g++, WSL gcc, clang-19) do NOT report it**, so three green legs and a 777-test suite were silent about it; the diagnosis is that aarch64 gcc inlines `computeDateTime` far enough up the `preprocess()` chain to see the bound, and the x86_64 legs do not. ⚠ **BE PRECISE ABOUT SEVERITY — the loose reading of this is wrong.** It is **NOT** a memory-safety bug and **NOT** an overflow: `snprintf` bounds its own write, so the only reachable harm is a TRUNCATED, i.e. WRONG, `__DATE__` string. It is also **not reachable from a sane clock** — `"Mmm dd yyyy"` is 11 chars + NUL and fits `char buf[16]` for every well-formed `tm`. **ROOT CAUSE is an INCONSISTENT DEFENSIVE POSTURE, and that is the real defect:** the function already range-checks `tm_mon` into `[0,12)` before indexing `kMon` — so its author was explicitly contemplating a malformed `tm` — yet passes `tm_mday` and `tm_year + 1900` straight through to `%2d`/`%04d`, where an out-of-range `int` needs up to 11 chars each and the format's true worst case is 3 + 1 + 11 + 1 + 11 + NUL = **28 bytes into a 16-byte buffer**. Guarding one field of a struct and not its siblings is the shape that rots. | **FIX (landed):** size both buffers for the format's WORST CASE over arbitrary `int` rather than for a well-formed date — `buf[32]` for `__DATE__` (28 needed) and `buf[40]` for `__TIME__` (three `%02d` at <=11 + two colons + NUL = 36) — so truncation is STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE instead of merely improbable. ⓘ **Chosen over clamping `tm_mday`/`tm_year` deliberately:** a clamp silently substitutes a wrong-but-plausible date and depends on the clamp staying correct, whereas correct sizing cannot be defeated by any input and needs no future maintenance; the `tm_mon` clamp stays because it guards an ARRAY INDEX, which sizing cannot help. ⚠ **REMAINING WORK — do not stamp this CLOSED until both are done: (1)** rebuild on the aarch64 host and confirm the warning is GONE (the fix was authored on an x86_64 host that never reproduced the warning, so it is currently UNVERIFIED against the only instrument that shows it — asserting otherwise would be exactly the error this registry exists to prevent); **(2)** decide whether the aarch64 leg should build `-Werror`, since a warning that only ONE host emits and no gate fails on is a warning nobody will read — that is the generalisable half, and it is worth more than this one buffer. | ✔MEASURED 2026-08-03 on the native arm64 VPS (aarch64 Ubuntu 24.04.4, gcc-13.3.0, Release) during the first native build of this project; warning text quoted verbatim above. ✔TREE `src/analysis/preprocess/preprocessor.cpp` (`MacroExpander::computeDateTime`). ★ **THE REUSABLE LESSON, which outlives the buffer:** a NEW HOST IS A NEW INSTRUMENT, and its first run is worth reading for what it says about the other legs — this warning had been latent through every green gate the project has ever run, and it took ninety minutes of arm64 hardware to surface. Expect more of these as the host is used; each is evidence about x86_64 coverage, not merely about arm64. · Sibling class [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (a green whose scope was narrower than it read) · [[D-HARNESS-CROSS-HOST-ANY-TARGET]] (the any-host requirement this host was commissioned to serve). |
| `D-CSUBSET-EXTERN-LIBRARY-SYNTAX-IS-NOT-VALID-C` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — HIGH — created 2026-08-10 (Lane M), found by the operator, who flagged the same file TWICE. ★★ THIS IS A REGRESSION THIS CYCLE INTRODUCED, NOT INHERITED LEGACY — and the first framing of it ("retire a legacy form") was wrong in a way that would have hidden the author.** ✔MEASURED (`git diff`, UNSTAGED ⇒ working-tree only, never in history): a lane REPLACED the standard-C spelling with the DSS-only one in two examples — `extern int _fmode;` / `extern char **_environ;` → `extern int _fmode "msvcrt.dll";` / `extern char **_environ "msvcrt.dll";` (`examples/c/extern_data_import_pe/main.c`) and `extern int _fmode;` → `extern int _fmode "msvcrt.dll";` (`examples/c/extern_data_addr_reject_pe/main.c`), under a comment asserting the form *"outranks the platform and is exactly the right thing here"*. **REVERTED 2026-08-10 (both files restored to HEAD, declarations AND the justifying prose).** ★ **THE FORM IS NOT VALID C, ON THE PRIMARY SOURCE:** ✔N3220 (C23) **§6.7.1** gives the COMPLETE production — `declaration: declaration-specifiers init-declarator-list_opt ;` with `init-declarator: declarator` OR `declarator = initializer` — so the ONLY thing admissible after a declarator is `= initializer`. A string literal is not in the grammar. ✔MEASURED independently (gcc 13.2.0 MinGW-W64-ucrt, `-std=c2x -pedantic-errors`, rc DIRECT): the string form → **rc=1** `error: expected '=', ',', ';', 'asm' or '__attribute__' before string constant`; the bare form → rc=0. (gcc's five-item list is WIDER than the standard's, because `asm` and `__attribute__` are gcc's own extensions — cite §6.7.1, not the error text.) ⇒ [[D-CSUBSET-EXTERN-LIBRARY-SYNTAX]] compiles under NO other toolchain, and against a FULL-C23 goal a corpus can only write the bare form. ★★ **THE REVERT REDS BOTH EXAMPLES, AND THAT IS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED:** ✔MEASURED on HEAD `3e86a187` + `build-dbg`, from the repo ROOT (a probe's config comes from the CWD), rc DIRECT, `--target x86_64:pe64-x86_64-windows-exec` — the reverted BARE source → **rc=1**, `error[K_SymbolUndefined] undefined symbol '_fmode'` AND `'_environ'`; the lane's `extern "lib"` source → rc=0. So the lane's FACTUAL claim was right (this cycle's retirement of `externLibraryByFormat` left the bare form unbound) and only its DESIGN conclusion was wrong. The examples stay red until a row lands — the fork below is an OPERATOR pick, deliberately not taken unilaterally. ★★ **THE BINDING MECHANISM EXISTS AND IS MEASURED — the lane's comment refuted its own premise.** It concluded that because the modern CRT reaches these *"only through accessors"* there is *"no data export to declare a row for"*; but **an accessor IS the descriptor row** — this cycle already shipped exactly that shape for the pe argv spine (`__p___argc`/`__p___argv`/`__p___wargv` + `_configure_narrow_argv`, `pe64-x86_64-windows-exec.format.json:52-56`), and `errno.json:10-17` ships `errno` → `(*_errno())` the same way. ✔MEASURED ON TWO INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS (`dumpbin /exports` MSVC 14.44.35207 AND `objdump -p` binutils 2.42, live `C:/Windows/System32/ucrtbase.dll`, 2,484 exports; ordinals stated as ORDINALS — `objdump`'s bracket number is a 0-BASED name-table index and PE OrdinalBase is 1): **`__p__fmode` ord 88 RVA 0x00099570 · `__p__environ` ord 87 RVA 0x00093370 · `_get_fmode` ord 320 · `_set_fmode` ord 1777 — ALL PRESENT, both instruments concurring.** ★★★ **BUT A BARE `extern int _fmode;` CAN NEVER BIND ON pe/UCRT, AND THE NEXT READER MUST NOT RE-ATTEMPT IT:** ✔MEASURED, both instruments, exact-name matcher, ZERO hits — **NEITHER `_fmode` NOR `_environ` IS A ucrtbase EXPORT AT ALL**, so no `kind: object` row is writable. The REAL SDK header AGREES rather than merely permitting it: `ucrt/stdlib.h:255` `#define _fmode (*__p__fmode())` with the direct `extern int _fmode;` at `:250` gated behind `_CRT_DECLARE_GLOBAL_VARIABLES_DIRECTLY` (a `ucrt.lib` STATIC shim, the `atexit`/`_crt_atexit` shape — not a DLL data export), and `ucrt/stdlib.h:1171` `#define _environ (*__p__environ())` where the direct arm at `:1168` is `#define _environ crt_usage_error__do_not_reference_global_pointer_directly__environ` — **UCRT ACTIVELY REFUSES to expose `_environ` as data.** Legacy `msvcrt.dll` DOES export both as DATA (`_fmode` ordinal 306 RVA 0x0009F24C, `_environ` ordinal 270 RVA 0x0009F8C8, both instruments concurring), but a SHIPPED row there re-imports the CRT this arc just retired and, since DSS eager-imports every declared shipped extern ([[D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-EAGER-IMPORT]]), pulls **TWO C RUNTIMES** into every program including the host header — the exact class [[D-CSUBSET-PE-BARE-EXTERN-STILL-MSVCRT-AFTER-UCRT-FLIP]] exists to eliminate — while ALSO making the witness semantically FALSE (msvcrt's `_fmode` is not the flag the image's ucrtbase stdio consults). ⓘ **INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED, DIFFERENT LANE, DIFFERENT CYCLE:** `unistd.json`'s `environ` clause records the same measurement with numbers MATCHING mine exactly (2,484 ucrtbase exports, `__p__environ` ord 87, msvcrt `_environ` ord 270) and the same verdict — *"ucrtbase exposes NO data export for it, permanently, exactly as `_fmode` goes through `_get_fmode`/`__p__fmode`"*. ★ **THE SECOND HALF OF THE PROBLEM, WHICH THE ACCESSOR FIX DOES NOT SOLVE:** ✔MEASURED — **all 7 shipped `kind: object` rows are gated `elf` or `macho`** (`stdio.json:82-87`, `unistd.json:44`), so **pe has ZERO data-object row** and `dataImportBinding: "got-indirect"` on pe is witnessed ONLY by these two examples. An accessor row does NOT restore that witness: `__p__fmode` is a FUNCTION import, so routing `_fmode` through it dissolves BOTH examples' subject (and `extern_data_addr_reject_pe` loses its subject entirely — there is no extern DATA import left to take the address of). A section-classifying sweep of all 2,484 ucrtbase exports (export RVA bucketed against the `objdump -h` section table, ImageBase 0x180000000) finds **EXACTLY TWO non-code exports** — `_mbcasemap` ord 527 RVA 0x001397E0 in **`.data` (WRITABLE)** and `_wctype` ord 1951 RVA 0x00100F30 in `.rdata` (READ-ONLY) — plus 13 `fothk` forwarder thunks (`ceil`/`floor`/`rint`/`trunc` families, still code). The real defect is that the form is non-standard and UNDOCUMENTED, not that it violates the invariant. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED** — the invalid-C form was in the working tree and is now reverted, leaving both examples red. ★ **OPERATOR FORK (pick before landing a row; the two are complementary, not alternatives — recommend BOTH):** **(A)** ship `__p__fmode` + `__p__environ` symbol rows (pe-gated) plus pe-gated macros `_fmode` → `(*__p__fmode())` and `_environ` → `(*__p__environ())` — verbatim what `ucrt/stdlib.h:255`/`:1171` do and verbatim the `errno.json` shape; consumers then reach them via `#include <stdlib.h>` with NO `extern`, which is 100% valid C. ⚠ This is the correct binding for those two NAMES but it does NOT restore the pe extern-DATA witness. **(B)** ship a pe-gated `kind: object` row for `_mbcasemap` and re-point BOTH examples at it with the BARE form `extern unsigned char _mbcasemap[];`, keeping their assertion ladders (they discriminate thunk-VA vs slot vs unfilled-slot and are the valuable part) — this is what keeps `dataImportBinding: got-indirect` witnessed by conforming C. ⚠⚠ **VERIFY BY EXECUTION, NEVER BY INSPECTION:** `unistd.json`'s `environ` clause records that a `kind: object` row produced a binary that LOOKED perfect (`readelf -r` showed `R_X86_64_COPY environ + 0`, dynsym showed an 8-byte OBJECT) and **READ NULL on all four legs**; a row that merely links is not a row that works. ★ **DECIDED 2026-08-10 — DO NOT RETIRE `extern "lib"`.** ✔MEASURED consumer set: 3 C files / 5 declarations (`examples/c/hello_writefile/main.c:55-57` `GetStdHandle`+`WriteFile` against `kernel32.dll`; the two above), 2 unit tests (`tests/analysis/syntactic/test_parser_c_subset_smoke.cpp:889`, `tests/hir/test_hir_lowering_c_subset.cpp:1622`), 3 example manifests; `real-examples/`, `integrated_tests/`, `tests/corpus/`, `scripts/` carry ZERO uses. ROOT CAUSE, read from the source rather than guessed: `shell.c:8228-8240` (the `generate_series` extension) selects its ceil/floor implementation from a three-arm ladder — `#if defined(SQLITE_ENABLE_MATH_FUNCTIONS) \|\| defined(_WIN32)` takes libm `ceil()`/`floor()` (Case 1); `#elif defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(SQLITE_DISABLE_INTRINSIC)` takes `__builtin_ceil`/`__builtin_floor` (Case 2); `#else` takes a home-grown integer routine (Case 3). Upstream wrote Case 3 precisely for a non-GCC compiler, and DSS opts out of that safety net by claiming to be GCC — the same honesty theme as [[D-CONFIG-COMMENT-CLAIM-ROT]]. **Trigger:** any upstream source whose `#if defined(__GNUC__)` arm names a `__builtin_*` DSS has not implemented; `__builtin_ceil`/`__builtin_floor` are merely the first two a real consumer reached, and more should be expected as the corpus grows. **Closing options — NOT decided, this needs a §B fork:** (1) implement both as REAL builtins lowering to the target's round-toward-±inf instruction (x86 `roundsd`, arm64 `frintp`/`frintm`) with a libm call fallback — best long-term, target-agnostic, and it makes the `__GNUC__` claim less of a lie; (2) implement them as always-injected aliases onto shipped libm `ceil`/`floor` — cheap, but no optimizer witness; (3) stop predefining `__GNUC__` — the most honest, but it would re-route a great deal of already-green upstream code and must be MEASURED before it is even contemplated. Option (1) is the recommendation. ⚠ DISCREPANCY WITH THIS CYCLE'S PREMISE, recorded rather than buried: the task that opened this row stated the elf CLI had been measured "clean except popen/pclose"; this cycle could NOT reproduce that — every elf probe run here also shows the ceil/floor pair. Whoever took the earlier measurement should reconcile it (most likely a different source staging, a different define set, or a probe that stopped at the link stage). | `D-FULLC-STDBIT-ADDRESSABLE-FN` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — RE-VERDICTED 2026-08-26 (cycle P40 lane J) FROM “⏳ DEFERRED, trigger-gated”. ★★★ IT IS NOT TRIGGER-GATED: THE TRIGGER ALREADY FIRED, AND THE BAR SAYS SO.** The row deferred this on *“no real consumer takes their address”* plus C23 §7.18.1p4's permission to define these as macros. Both are true and BOTH ARE BESIDE THE POINT under `DSS = (gcc ∪ clang ∪ MSVC) ∪ ISO C`, which binds in the direction of ACCEPTANCE: ✔**MEASURED 2026-08-26, probing the references SEPARATELY — gcc 13.3.0 AND clang 18.1.3 (Ubuntu, glibc `<stdbit.h>`) BOTH COMPILE, LINK AND RUN `fp p = &stdc_leading_zeros_ui; p(0x00FFFFFF)` and both print 8.** Two of three references accept a construct DSS refuses ⇒ this is a CONFORMANCE DIVERGENCE, schedulable now, not a nicety waiting for a consumer. ⚠ The general lesson: *a trigger phrased as “when a user needs it” cannot be discharged by looking at our own corpus* — the reference union is the trigger, and it had been met since C23 support landed. ★ **WHAT THE REFERENCES ACTUALLY DO, measured by reading glibc's header rather than inferring:** each name is declared as a REAL EXTERN FUNCTION (`extern unsigned int stdc_leading_zeros_ui (unsigned int) __THROW __attribute_const__;`) **AND** shadowed by a same-named function-like macro. Because a function-like macro is only invoked when followed by `(`, the ordinary call form takes the macro and `&name` takes the declaration — which resolves to a real symbol in libc. ★ So the mechanism is not “expose the builtins as addressable” (the second option this row named); it is **a real definition per name, shadowed by the existing macro**. ★★ **AND IT IS LARGER THAN THIS ROW IMPLIED — SIZED, NOT GUESSED.** ✔MEASURED: (1) `stdbit.json` is macros-ONLY — 88 macro rows and not one `symbols` entry; (2) the descriptor root vocabulary (`readShippedLibDescriptor`'s `rejectUnknownKeys`) has **no key that carries a function BODY** — a `symbols` row carries a `signature`, a DECLARATION; (3) the only shape that ships a real definition is `realization: {"<format>": {"source": "runtime/platform/src/….c"}}`, compiled as an ordinary extra TU — and it has **exactly two users, `dirent.json` and `unistd.json`, BOTH `pe`-only**, so an all-three-formats `realization` is new ground; (4) there is **no precedent anywhere for a shipped function whose body calls a `__builtin_*`** — `grep -rn "__builtin_" src/dss-config/runtime/` is EMPTY; (5) the surface is ~70 wrappers (14 ops × 4 concrete widths + 14 per-format `_ul`), each needing the macro and the symbol to coexist under one name, which the descriptor loader has never been asked to do. ⇒ **a cycle-sized arc, not a lane-slice**, and it owes a decision the operator may want to make: whether the wrappers ride a new `realization` TU or whether `symbols` gains a body-bearing form. ✔MEASURED, the trigger has fired NOWHERE IN THIS REPO — `grep -rn "&stdc_" examples/ tests/ src/` is empty and `examples/c/c23_stdbit` never takes an address — which is why it stayed invisible; the reference probe is what found it. Priority: **normal** (a real divergence, but every mainstream-observable call form is already correct, and nothing in the sqlite corpus takes one of these addresses). ⚠ **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (cycle P41, lane P) — THE FIRST BLOCKER IS CLEARED, AND THE NEXT ONE IS NAMED AND MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED.** ✔MEASURED end-to-end through the CLI on a `$DSS_CONFIG_ROOT` copy of `stdbit.json` carrying `symbols` rows for one operation's four concrete widths: the PRE-FIX `dsscp` REFUSED THE DESCRIPTOR AT LOAD on the macro/symbol shadowing guard — that guard, not the body-source question, is what stopped this row FIRST — and the POST-FIX `dsscp` LOADS PAST IT (see [[D-FFI-MACRO-SHADOWING-GUARD-IGNORES-C-7-1-4-FUNCTION-ADDRESSABILITY]], closed the same cycle). The NEXT error is `H_UnsupportedLoweringForKind` — *“`importLibrary` is missing from the HirAttribute&lt;FfiMetadata&gt; side-table”*, once per ExternFunction, then `H0009 Ref to unbound symbol` — i.e. exactly the body-source decision this row already names: stdbit has no `library`, so the bodies must come from a `realization`-reached shipped source or a body-bearing `symbols` form. ✔The four flat spellings PARSE AND LOAD (the descriptor reached HIR). Full surface: 14 ops × 4 concrete widths = 56 flat rows plus 14 `_ul` rows carrying the LP64/LLP64 divergence through the existing `stdio.json` `fseek`/`ftell` `signatureByDataModel` mechanism, ~70 rows; the generic `stdc_<op>(x)` forms need NO symbol row, being `_Generic` macros over the concrete names, which is what glibc does too.| Ship a real addressable definition per `stdc_<op>_<T>`, shadowed by the existing macro exactly as glibc does. Decide FIRST which surface carries the body — a `runtime/platform/src/stdbit.c` reached by an all-three-formats `realization` (new: both existing users are `pe`-only, and no runtime TU has ever called a builtin), or a body-bearing form on `symbols`. Then: the ~70 wrappers, the macro/symbol same-name coexistence through the descriptor loader, `ffi::ShippedTypeConsistency` acceptance, and a corpus example that takes `&stdc_leading_zeros_ui`, calls THROUGH the pointer, and also uses the parenthesized form `(stdc_leading_zeros_ui)(x)` — both of which are red today. Pin against the measured reference behaviour (gcc AND clang both return 8). | FC17.9(b) (opened by [[D-FULLC-STDBIT]]); `shippedLibs/stdbit.json`; the `realization` precedent in `shippedLibs/{dirent,unistd}.json` + `src/dss-config/runtime/platform/src/`; `src/ffi/shipped_lib_descriptor.cpp` `readShippedLibDescriptor`; bar: the reference compilers are the spec (`DSS = (gcc ∪ clang ∪ MSVC) ∪ ISO C`); plan 23 |

| `D-CSUBSET-ENUM-UNDERLYING-TYPE-QUALIFIERS` | P2 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — the gate has FIRED and this row is SCHEDULABLE NOW: opened 2026-07-09 by [[D-CSUBSET-ENUM-UNDERLYING-TYPE]], whose status cell reads CLOSED as of 2026-09-03.** A `const`/`volatile`-qualified underlying type on the clause — `enum E : const unsigned char { … }`. The clause is `[Colon, castTypeBase]` (the qualifier-free base type-name), so a qualifier before the base is NOT admitted → the speculative clause does not match and the parse rejects it (LOUD, never a silent drop of the qualifier). C23 permits any integer type-specifier-qualifier sequence; the qualifier is semantically irrelevant to the underlying (an enum's underlying is an rvalue integer). **Trigger:** real source writes a qualified underlying type. Resolution: widen `enumTypeSpecifier`'s base from `castTypeBase` to a qualifier-admitting form (e.g. the head-qualifier `{repeat headQualifier}` prefix castTypeRef uses) and strip the (ignored) qualifier at resolve. | (gated — trivial widen; no shipped consumer writes a qualified enum underlying) | opened by the enum-underlying cycle; `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json` (`enumTypeSpecifier` base) |

C23 §6.7.2.2 permits both (`char` and `bool` ARE integer types), but DSS's `isIntegerKind` (the underlying-type gate) is `I8..U128` only — it EXCLUDES `Char`/`Bool`/`Byte`, consistent with DSS's deliberate "`Char`/`Bool` are NOT arithmetic-integer ranks" posture (they reach the int ranks only via the `charConvertsToArith`/`boolWidensToArith` config gates, not natively). So `enum E : char` is REJECTED with `S_InvalidEnumUnderlyingType` (LOUD, never a silent wrong-width enum). **Trigger:** real source needs a char/bool-underlying enum, OR native Char/Bool-as-integer-rank support lands. Resolution: admit `Char`/`Bool` in the enum-underlying gate + map to their byte width in `enumeratorValueFitsUnderlying`, once they participate in the integer ranks. | (gated — a loud rejection today; consistent with the Char/Bool non-arithmetic-rank posture) | opened by the enum-underlying cycle (code-audit); `src/analysis/semantic/semantic_analyzer.cpp` (`isIntegerKind` underlying gate) + `type_rules.hpp` (`enumeratorValueFitsUnderlying`) |

Unshadowing just FOUR real headers moved the corpus 126 → 159. **★ REVERSE HAZARD, and TF-C113 CREATED IT:** when both models reach ONE TU they CONFLICT — 10× `P0014 incompatible redefinition of macro: INT8_C` (`stdint.json:69` defines `INT8_C(v)`→`v`; Apple `stdint.h:62` defines it →`(v)`), while the real header ALONE is clean. So TF-C113's six Darwin descriptors do not merely make those headers unreadable on a native Mac, they make MIXED builds actively hazardous — a larger cost than the "accepted deliberately" note in that commit judged. **★ FOUR PREDICTED BLOCKERS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT (recorded so they cannot be re-proposed as suspects):** (a) bounds-safety attributes — `malloc/_ptrcheck.h:32-36` defines `__counted_by`/`__sized_by`/`__unsafe_indexable` to nothing absent `__has_ptrcheck`, they never reach the parser; (b) ptrauth / `MALLOC_ZONE_FN_PTR` — neutralised at `malloc/malloc.h:58-59`, and the REAL `malloc/malloc.h` is CLEAN; (c) availability macros — `API_AVAILABLE(...)` parses fine across 144 headers; (d) nullability — `cdefs.h:273`/`:283` define `_Nullable`/`_Nonnull` away. All four were INFERRED from raw header text; the preprocessor had already handled every one. **★ TWO COUNTING TRAPS, both MEASURED:** one construct emits ~4 diagnostics as the parser resyncs, so RANK BY ROOT SITE and never by diagnostic count; and semantic diagnostics are GATED on a clean parse — `math.h` alone yields 51 `S*`, the same TU plus ONE syntax error yields 1 `P0009` and ZERO `S*` ⇒ every total UNDER-counts, never over-counts. **REAL blockers, ranked by root-site leverage:** (i) ✅ **DONE 2026-08-04 (TF-C115) — see [[D-PP-ENDIANNESS-PREDEFINES]]; and this item's own estimate here was WRONG, which is recorded rather than quietly amended.** It read "missing `__LITTLE_ENDIAN__`/`__BIG_ENDIAN__` predefines → `#error Unknown endianess` at `OSByteOrder.h:314`, **ROOTS 11 HEADERS**, config-only, cheapest on the list". ✘The "11 headers" figure **does not reproduce against the 202-header top-level corpus this row's headline numbers are measured on** — only **2** of the 202 reach an endianness `#error` root, and that corpus moved 126→126 clean / 1419→1417 diagnostics. ✔The payoff is real but lives in a DIFFERENT DENOMINATOR and is much larger: of 1708 SDK headers, **63** transitively reach the root, and on that set headers emitting the `#error` went **60 → 0**, clean 1 → 7, zero regressions. ★ LESSON FOR THE REMAINING ITEMS ON THIS LIST: a root-site leverage count is only meaningful WITH ITS DENOMINATOR NAMED, and every other estimate below inherits that caveat — none of them has been checked against the wider SDK. Also closed a SILENT defect nobody had predicted: `#if __BYTE_ORDER__ == __ORDER_BIG_ENDIAN__` was `0 == 0` and therefore TRUE on every little-endian leg, so the unshipped state was SELECTING the big-endian arm rather than merely failing to answer. And a new front blocker was EXPOSED by the fix — `__builtin_constant_p`, [[D-CSUBSET-BUILTIN-CONSTANT-P]] — so 5 headers went 1 → 6 diagnostics; that is PROGRESS surfacing the next layer, not a regression, and the honest metric is headers-emitting-the-root-error. Explicit opt-in turns "can DSS consume a vendor SDK" into a SUPPORTED, DECLARED path rather than an accident of what is on the machine, and it keeps the measured 159/202 capability reachable without ever letting install state change a build result. Implementation must stay target-keyed and config-driven — the opt-in is a DECLARATION, never a host probe, and never an `if (sdkPresent)`. That is strictly worse than a refusal and it is exactly the outcome `provider: "host-system"` currently prevents by refusing to build at all. ✔THE COMPILER ITSELF IS FILE-FREE, MEASURED: P1 macho arm64 exec, libc only → rc=0, 49,845 B, one `LC_LOAD_DYLIB`; P2 with `extern … "libtcl8.6.dylib"`/`"libz.1.dylib"` → rc=0, **3 LC_LOAD_DYLIBs, `_Tcl_Eval`→ord 2, `_compress2`→ord 3**; `src/link/format/macho.cpp:3257-3293` builds the dylib set from `schema.loadDylibs ∪ distinct externImports[].libraryPath`, `:3306` resolves the ordinal by `std::string` compare, `:4586` writes the string verbatim — and there is **no `ifstream`/`fopen`/`exists` anywhere in `src/link/`**. ✔P4: `--resolve-library` READ A REAL DARWIN ARM64 DYLIB **ON WINDOWS** (export table + `LC_ID_DYLIB`), so the reader is already host-independent. ⇒ the library file is consumed as the source of the **symbol→install-name binding and nothing else**. ✔THE CONTRACT IS NARROW, which is why a declaration can satisfy it: the whole channel produces one row type, `ffi::ImportSurface` (`src/ffi/import_surface.hpp`) — `mangledName`, `libraryPath`, `soname`, `kind`, `visibility`, `linkage`, `forwardTarget` — and `ffi::ingest()` (`ingest.cpp:354-396`) sets `meta.importLibrary = soname.empty() ? libraryPath : soname`. **It never reads a signature.** Types come from the source's own declaration via the real `-I` header ⇒ **a declared-library input carries NO type or layout burden at all**, which is the decisive asymmetry with a descriptor. **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: HIGH. | Add a **per-leg DECLARED LIBRARY INPUT**, living with the consumer (`real-examples/c/sqlite/libdecl/<lib>.<format>.json`, referenced from each leg's `libraries` block) and NEVER in `src/dss-config/` per [[D-CONFIG-SHIPPEDLIBS-OS-ONLY-RULE-WAS-UNWRITTEN]]. It enters through the EXISTING `--resolve-library` magic-byte dispatch (`src/ffi/binary_reader.cpp` `guessFormat`/`readImportsFromBytes`) as one more arm, gated on a REQUIRED `"dssLibraryDeclarationVersion": 1` key — fail loud without it, never a bare `{` sniff. No new CLI flag; static, dynamic and declared all arrive through the one channel. Contents: soname/install name + symbol list + optional per-symbol kind/linkage/visibility/version + PROVENANCE (which real library, which host, when). ★★ **THE VERIFICATION HALF IS MANDATORY AND THE MECHANISM MUST NOT SHIP WITHOUT IT: a declaration can LIE, and a lie costs a LOAD failure (`0xC0000139` / exit 127), not a build failure. The measurement that justified the rejection is unchanged. ⏳ **OPEN — gate WITHDRAWN 2026-08-23 (P29 lane E), created 2026-08-14 by the operator ruling that shipped positional operand selectors (plan 29 §4.7). This row exists so the REJECTED alternative is recorded with a testable re-entry condition, not so it becomes a backlog item.** Positional selectors let a dialect declare that an operand token **selects the opcode** and is consumed by the match — closing `mrs <Xd>, cntvct_el0` and `cset <Xd>, <cc>` without any sysreg vocabulary. The alternative — modelling system registers as a genuine operand kind with a target-declared sysreg table and a real encoding slot — was **REJECTED, and NOT merely as a speculative build.** ✔MEASURED both shipped targets: x86_64 `rdtsc` and arm64 `cntvct` are **BOTH zero-operand with WHICH COUNTER baked into the opcode** (they differ in result model — fixed `rax:rdx` pair vs `Xd` — and that difference is real and fine). ⇒ *"read the hardware counter"* is **ONE VERB across ISAs**, and `cntvct_el0` is an AArch64 spelling detail exactly as `edx:eax` is an x86 one. Making the arm64 read a generic `mrs` + sysreg operand would give it a **different SHAPE from the x86 read**, so every shared consumer — starting with `hwtime.h`'s own lowering — would acquire a per-arch shape distinction: **the `if (arch == …)` the bar hard-vetoes, arriving through CONFIG rather than C++, which is the slower and worse way for it to arrive.** ★ The opcode's own `$comment` already made this argument: *"a generic `mrs` could only ever read one system register."* ★★ **SELECTORS FORECLOSE NOTHING** — if the trigger below fires, a real sysreg operand kind **coexists** with selector rows, the selector row being a **SPECIALIZATION** (the same relation `nop` has to a generic form) — *provided* the load-time ambiguity refusal stays in place, which is the second reason that refusal is not optional. ⚠ **HONESTY CLAUSE:** the selector mechanism's two witnesses (`mrs`, `cset`) are both in **ONE dialect**. Per this project's own standard — *"a mechanism witnessed by one signatory is the weaker claim this project keeps having to walk back"* — two rows in one dialect is stronger than one and weaker than two dialects. Do not present it as settled cross-dialect merely because the key is shared-loader-owned. | **Trigger — TESTABLE, not a vibe: a shipped target requires an `mrs`/`msr` whose system register is NOT one of a small set of concrete DSS verbs — i.e. the sysreg must be carried as DATA into a real encoding slot.** Until that fires, building it is the speculative build §A.2 forbids, AND it would break the agnosticism above. **Closing work when it fires:** a target-declared system-register table + a real `EncodingSlotKind` for the 15-bit sysreg field, coexisting with (not replacing) the selector rows; the shipped zero-operand `cntvct`/`tlsbase` rows must keep working unchanged, since they are the specialization. ⚠ Note the earlier refutation (plan 29 §4, handoff §4.1) remains CORRECT about the ENCODING side — `cntvct` needs zero new slot vocabulary — so this trigger is about a *second, genuinely data-carrying* sysreg, never about `cntvct` itself. | `src/dss-config/sources/asm-arm64-gas.lang.json` · `src/dss-config/targets/arm64.target.json` (`cntvct`, `tlsbase`) · `src/core/types/assembly_config.hpp` (`operandSelectors`) · plan 29 §4.7 · supersedes the closed [[D-ASM-ARM64-SYSTEM-REGISTER-AS-OPERAND-UNMODELLED]] |
