| `D-LK-MERGED-FOREIGN-FUNCTIONS-CARRY-NO-UNWIND-INFO-IN-THE-IMAGE` | P1 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — NARROWED TO arm64 MACH-O AND PE: THE MACH-O x86_64 HALF NOW CARRIES, AND THE VOCABULARY BLOCKER THAT HELD IT IS GONE (P34 lane C, 2026-08-25).** ✔MEASURED end-to-end on the operator's own Apple Silicon (Darwin 25.5.0 arm64, macOS 26.5.2, Apple clang 21.0.0 / clang-2100.1.1.101) with a fixture built by asking Apple's compiler for NOTHING — `/usr/bin/cc -arch x86_64 -c` then `/usr/bin/ar rcs` (member md5 `6f76f829cf79c3ee9d6cb08ca7df0e8a`, 952 B; archive `5817f704cc1060c1c55b41b8c8337264`, 1216 B): DSS **on Windows** links `main` against that archive and the resulting Mach-O exec (md5 `a9e7277bd2d0a5e412a596f944442d14`) carries a `__TEXT,__eh_frame` of 0xac bytes holding **FOUR** FDEs — DSS's own `_dss_macho_unwind_leaf` and `_main`, and the TWO MERGED FOREIGN functions `_dss_macho_foreign_inner` `0x1000010a5..0x100001112` (109 bytes) and `_dss_macho_foreign_outer` `0x100001115..0x10000113c` (39 bytes), each FDE beginning at its symbol and carrying the EXACT extent the member's own FDE stated (`pc=0..0x6d` and `pc=0x70..0x97`). **Apple's OWN `dwarfdump --eh-frame` reads them back off the image** and renders the merged function's per-PC state as the rules clang originally stated: `0x1000010a5: CFA=RSP+8: RIP=[CFA-8]` → `0x1000010a6: CFA=RSP+16: RBP=[CFA-16], RIP=[CFA-8]` → `0x1000010a9: CFA=RBP+16: RBP=[CFA-16], RIP=[CFA-8]`; `nm -n` shows both foreign symbols MERGED into `__text` (`T`, not imported); and the image **RUNS NATIVELY on that Mac, exit 42** (md5 re-verified on arrival). THE COUNTERFACTUAL IS A CONFIG MUTANT WITH NOTHING REBUILT — `dsscp.exe` `4e588777739d66ff68b4734bf6d2db34` and `libdsscp.dll` `a48977e88ee260d278f30358f6da8d7d` byte-identical either side: delete the `dwarf-cfi` unwind row from the two x86_64 relocatable documents and the image drops **4 FDEs → 2** (DSS's own only, `__eh_frame` 0xac → 0x6c, md5 `043441e5c99325c6e563e474ab740bc6`, still exit 42) with the reader's warning back naming 2 undescribed functions; restore the documents and the relink is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the original. ✔The `--config=release` image is byte-identical to the baseline, which is why the corpus example's `release` arm is deliberately NOT armed with `mustDifferFromBaseline`. ★★ **THE BLOCKER WAS VOCABULARY, AND THE RULING IS IMPLEMENTED AS GIVEN: A DISCRIMINATING FIELD ON THE SECTION ROW, NEVER A SECOND `SectionKind`.** ✔MEASURED 2026-08-25, `/usr/bin/cc -arch <a> -c foreign.c` with no other flag: the x86_64 object carries `__LD,__compact_unwind` (0x40 bytes, align 2^3, nreloc 2) **AND** `__TEXT,__eh_frame` (0x68 bytes, align 2^3, flags 0x6800000B), while the arm64 object from the SAME compiler and the SAME source carries compact ONLY — two sections, one ROLE, two ENCODINGS. New `SectionEncoding {Unspecified, DwarfCfi, CompactUnwind}` in `section_kind.hpp`; `ObjectFormatSectionInfo::encoding`; the row identity is now the `(kind, encoding)` PAIR and `sectionKindIndex` keys on it. ⚠ **STILL OPEN ON arm64 MACH-O AND ON PE.** arm64: a compact record is `{fnStart, len, encoding, personality, lsda}` with **NO PC DIMENSION**, so an FDE built from one would assert a rule clang never stated for every prologue PC; its honest carry is the `__TEXT,__unwind_info` SYNTHESIS this row already names. PE: unchanged from P33 — the Win64 decode is faithful but the vocabulary lands three gates deep in the COFF reader. | ★★★ **THE MACH-O CARRY ADDS NO EMIT PATH ANYWHERE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A ROW OF CONFIG PLUS A DISPATCH.** DWARF CFI is the encoding `dwarf_cfi_decode.hpp` (P33) already inverts into the neutral `CfiFunction`, and `macho.cpp`'s `(c.5)` block already builds `__TEXT,__eh_frame` from `AssembledFunction::cfi` — so declaring `__TEXT,__eh_frame` as a second `unwind` row IS the fix, and zero new writer code exists. The reader dispatches on the DECLARED ENCODING and never on the section NAME; a third encoding (ARM EHABI, Win64 SEH) is a config row plus a decoder, not a core enum edit. ★ **FOUR SCHEMA RULES KEEP `sectionByKind` HONEST**, because it is the kind-only lookup every WRITER uses and it cannot name an encoding: the `(kind, encoding)` pair must be unique (the message names BOTH halves, since "duplicate kind" alone would send an author to delete the row that is legitimately there); a kind may repeat only where `sectionKindIsEncodingDiscriminated` accepts it (✔MEASURED that `SectionKind::Unwind` appears in the two relocatable READERS and in NO writer); every row of a repeated kind must declare its encoding (pair-uniqueness alone would admit one silent row beside one declared one, and the silent row is exactly the unidentifiable one); and an `encoding` on a kind that has only one is refused as inert config, by name, exactly as `charSignedness` refuses a second-owner key. `Unspecified` is the ABSENT key and is NOT spellable — the `kSelectableObjectFormatKindNames` sentinel shape, with a `static_assert` pinning the rejected count. `ObjectFormatData::addSectionRow` is the ONE owner of both derived indexes, so a hand-built schema in `tests/` cannot maintain them differently. ★★ **THREE DESIGN CLAIMS WERE MEASURED FALSE BY REAL APPLE BYTES, and every one of them would have shipped a reader that bound nothing or refused everything.** (1) **`__TEXT,__eh_frame` CARRIES NO RELOCATIONS** — nreloc 0 on the fixture AND on a fresh object. The ELF reader binds each FDE through `.rela.eh_frame` because its `initial_location` is ZERO; Mach-O stores a **pcrel delta resolved inside the object's own flat address space**, the opposite convention, so the binding resolves `sectionAddr + fieldOffset + storedDelta` and REFUSES a relocation-bearing DWARF unwind section rather than bind on half a reference. (2) **APPLE'S CIE DECLARES POINTER ENCODING 0x10** (`pcrel\|absptr`, an EIGHT-byte field), not gcc's `0x1b` (`pcrel\|sdata4`) — the decoder refused it outright, which is the fail-loud arm working, and the repair is one constant plus `dwEhPeIsEightByteField`/`dwEhPeIsPcRelative` so the GATE and the READER cannot disagree about a field's width. (3) **THE ELF READER'S EXACT-EQUALITY EXTENT RULE IS WRONG ON MACH-O** and refused the ordinary output of the platform's own compiler: ✔MEASURED with `otool -tvV`, `_dss_foreign_leaf` is FIFTEEN bytes of code (`retq` at 0x0e) followed by a `nop` at 0x0f, and the next function starts at 0x10 because `__text` has align 2^4 — so BOTH of Apple's encodings say 15 while the atom this reader slices says 16, because under MH_SUBSECTIONS_VIA_SYMBOLS an atom runs to the NEXT SYMBOL and swallows the padding. The rule is now stated against the DANGER instead of the number: the FDE may not exceed the atom, and rounding its extent up to the SECTION'S DECLARED ALIGNMENT must reach the atom's end — not a tolerance, since an FDE describing 3 bytes of a 100-byte function still fails. ★ **TWO ORDERED PASSES, AND THE ORDER WAS A DEFECT CAUGHT BY RUNNING IT:** `__LD,__compact_unwind` sits at a LOWER address than `__TEXT,__eh_frame` in every Apple x86_64 object, so a single wire-order loop asked "how many functions are still undescribed" BEFORE the DWARF arm had described any and warned about three functions that were about to be covered. Carrying now finishes before anything reports what was not carried, and the warning counts FUNCTIONS THAT REACH THE IMAGE UNDESCRIBED rather than records — the record count alone was a false alarm on the leg whose sibling section carries, and a false alarm on the common case is how a reader learns to ignore the one that is real. An unwind row with NO declared encoding is REFUSED by the reader, never defaulted: reading a compact body as DWARF resynchronizes onto record bytes and reports a confident table of noise, which is worse than the silence this row exists to end. **WITNESSES:** 15 tests in `tests/link/test_macho_compact_unwind_section.cpp` over the two checked-in REAL STOCK Apple objects, the two legs now asserting OPPOSITE outcomes from one shared body (x86_64: every function carries `cfi`, the decoded entry state is pinned at CFA offset 8 with the return address at CFA-8, the three-op `push rbp; mov rsp,rbp` prologue is pinned op-by-op INCLUDING its pcOffsets, `validateCfiFunction` — the invariant every format's encoder spends — accepts the result, and the whole read is SILENT; arm64: nothing carried, exactly one warning naming 3 undescribed functions). **9/9 MUTANTS RED, EXERCISED NOT READ**, each with build rc=0 and the subject TU's OWN `.obj` md5 moving and restoring byte-identically, plus the config-level mutant above run through the shipped CLI. ⚠⚠ **AND THE MUTATION HARNESS ITSELF HAD TO BE REPAIRED MID-RUN, WHICH IS THE SECOND INSTRUMENT DEFECT THIS ROW HAS PAID FOR:** its pristine-copy loop read a Python-generated path list that arrives CRLF on Windows, bash's IFS does not split on a carriage return, and `cp` was never checked — so every backup but the last silently failed, every RESTORE silently failed, and mutants 2..8 all ran with their predecessors STILL LIVE. It gave itself away by the failure lists ACCUMULATING and by "restored md5 != before" on every mutant; the v2 harness checks `cp`, strips the carriage return, asserts the marker is gone from the SOURCE, and then RE-RUNS ctest and requires GREEN — a control which immediately caught one further mutant still live that a grep sweep had missed. It also corrected a subject: a HEADER has no object of its own, so `dwarf_cfi_decode.hpp`'s md5 comparison was md5-of-nothing against md5-of-nothing and reported INVALID rather than a false RED; its subject is now the object of a TU that includes it. ✔The compiler is REPRODUCIBLE on this host — the restored `.obj` md5 returns exactly — so the earlier mismatches were the failed restore and nothing else. ⚠ A THIRD instrument defect of this lane's own, and it belongs beside the CRLF one because it is the SAME trap wearing different clothes: a Python `open(p,'w')` WITHOUT `newline=''` translates every newline on Windows, so one edit silently rewrote the whole of `section_kind.hpp` to CRLF — invisible in `git diff` (which normalises and read 153/0) and visible only in the on-disk bytes. Normalised back to LF, and ✔MEASURED that all THREE subject objects come back BYTE-IDENTICAL to the values the mutant table records, so that table still describes the tree that ships. ⚠ **THE CORPUS EXAMPLE'S HONEST LIMIT, in the manifest and in `main.c`:** `examples/c/macho_merged_foreign_unwind_carry` witnesses that the carry does not CORRUPT the link or the run, not the WALK — no shipped library descriptor exposes `backtrace` or `_Unwind_Backtrace`, the same measured limit its ELF sibling records — and its `containerWitness` is the literal `__eh_frame`, so a member rebuilt with `-fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables` fails the manifest rather than passing vacuously (✔EXERCISED: pointing it at an absent section reddens the in-process runner). ⚠ On a Windows host only the in-process runner compiles it, because the CLI-subprocess sibling builds only host-runnable specs — the documented boundary its ELF sibling sits on too. **HALF TWO IS NOT DONE AND IS NOT REFUSED-BUT-CLAIMED:** the arm64 `__TEXT,__unwind_info` synthesis is BLOCKED ON OWNERSHIP AND ON A HAZARD, both measured. A compact record's `{encoding, personality, lsda}` has no carrier from reader to writer — `AssembledFunction` and `AssembledModule` both live in `src/asm/asm.hpp`, and the only other route is `src/program/compile_pipeline.cpp`, neither of which this lane owns. The real ceiling on a `*&` chain — a shape that does reach it — is **100 rc 0 / 200 SEGFAULT**, the lowest anywhere in the MIR tier, ahead of the `SehTryExcept` arm this row called lowest and ahead of the `SeqExpr` sites. ⇒ **A ceiling is a claim about a ROUTE: confirm the construct reaches the site (flatten sets included) before recording a number for it.** ⚠ **AND THE `SeqExpr` FIGURE WAS AN UNTESTED UPPER BOUND, NOT A CEILING** — `200 rc 0` above is real but is not a wall; the wall is **300 rc 0 / 400 SEGFAULT**, and its axis is **LIST LENGTH**, which is precisely the sqlite shape this row's own ranking analysis identifies as the reachable one. ⛔ **STILL OPEN AND DELIBERATELY DECLINED, WITH THE SHAPE OF THE FIX STATED**: the three `SeqExpr` sites, because the death is a six-frame `SeqExpr` ⇄ `ExprStmt` ⇄ `AssignStmt` cycle (gdb-attributed) and an alternating cycle only flattens when **every** edge sits on one stack — the statement driver, the expression driver and `lowerDiscardedExpr`'s driver must be merged, a plan-24-Stage-4-scale change.
